&\displaystyle\sup_{\alpha\neq 0}\frac{\|\big(\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{[\vartheta]}u_{R,x_{0}}(t,x)\big)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\alpha|^{\vartheta-[\vartheta]}},\ \ \ \vartheta\notin\mathbb{N}.\end{cases}

We first prove the following lemma to estimate
u_{L,x_{0}}
and
u_{N,x_{0}}
.

Lemma 2.5

Let
T>0
. For any
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,
h:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, and let
\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x)
be the kernel defined in (
2.4
). Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}[g](t,x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,0,x-y)g(y)dy,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-y)h(\tau,y)dyd\tau.

Then the following estimates hold.

i) For any
\sigma_{1}\geq 0
,
\sigma_{2}>0
, it holds

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{t>0}\|\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}[g](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{1}}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{1}}},

(2.21)

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{t>0}t^{\frac{\sigma_{2}}{s}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}[g](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}}\lesssim_{\sigma_{2}}\|g\|_{\dot{B}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\infty,\infty}},

(2.22)

ii) For any
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}
satisfying
0<\sigma_{3}<s
,
\sigma_{3}+\sigma_{4},\sigma_{3}+\sigma_{4}-s\notin\mathbb{N}
and
\sigma_{4}>s-\sigma_{3}
, and any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{0,n+\sigma_{3}\}}\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{a}{s}}

\displaystyle(\|\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{4}+a}}+\|\Lambda^{\sigma_{4}}\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{a}})

(2.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(t^{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{3}+\sigma_{4}-s}}+t^{\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\sigma_{3}+\sigma_{4}-s}}\right).

Proof.
By (
2.16
), (
2.17
), and Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}[g](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{1}}}\lesssim\|\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,0)\|_{L^{1}}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{1}}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{1}}}.

This implies (
2.21
). The estimate (
2.22
) follows from Proposition
6.4
.

Then we prove (
2.23
). Denote
\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma_{3}+\sigma_{4}-s\notin\mathbb{N}
. Since
\sigma_{4}>\tilde{\sigma}
,

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\sigma_{4}}\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Lambda^{\sigma_{4}-[\tilde{\sigma}]}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)(\Lambda^{[\tilde{\sigma}]}h(\tau,z)-\Lambda^{[\tilde{\sigma}]}h(\tau,x))dzd\tau.

Applying Corollary
2.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\Lambda^{\sigma_{4}}\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t,x)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\Lambda^{\sigma_{4}-[\tilde{\sigma}]}K_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||x-z|^{\tilde{\sigma}-[\tilde{\sigma}]}dz\|h(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}d\tau

(2.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{s-\sigma_{3}}{s}}\tau^{-\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}d\tau\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}.

Note that for
\sigma_{4}\notin\mathbb{N}
, there holds

\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{4}}}=\|f\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{\sigma_{4}}}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{\sigma_{4}}f\|_{L^{\infty}},

and the estimate (
2.24
) still holds if
\Lambda^{\sigma_{4}}
is replaced by
\nabla^{\sigma_{4}}
for
\sigma_{4}\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\|\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\sigma_{4}}}+\|\Lambda^{\sigma_{4}}\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t)\|_{L^{\infty}})\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}.

(2.25)

Then we estimate the higher order norm. Assume
n+\sigma_{3}+\sigma_{4}=\tilde{n}+\gamma
, where
\tilde{n}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
. Let
\tilde{\gamma}\in(\max\{\gamma-s,0\},\gamma)
. Using integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{\tilde{n}}\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t,x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{\tilde{n}}\Lambda^{-(\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma})}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||\Lambda^{\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma}}h(\tau,z)-\Lambda^{\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma}}h(\tau,x)|dzd\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{\tilde{n}-n}\Lambda^{-(\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma})}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||\nabla^{n}\Lambda^{\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma}}h(\tau,z)-\nabla^{n}\Lambda^{\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma}}h(\tau,x)|dzd\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{\tilde{n}}\Lambda^{-(\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma})}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||x-z|^{\tilde{\gamma}}dz\,\|h(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{\tilde{n}-n}\Lambda^{-(\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma})}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||x-z|^{\tilde{\gamma}}dz\,\|h(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\tilde{\sigma}}}d\tau

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{I}_{1}+\mathcal{I}_{2}.

From Corollary
2.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{\tilde{n}}\Lambda^{-(\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma})}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||x-z|^{\tilde{\gamma}}dz\lesssim|\alpha|^{\gamma}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{n+s}{s}},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{\tilde{n}-n}\Lambda^{-(\tilde{\sigma}-\tilde{\gamma})}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||x-z|^{\tilde{\gamma}}dz\lesssim|\alpha|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\min\left\{1,\frac{|\alpha|}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{1}{s}}}\right\}^{1-\tilde{\gamma}}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{s-(\gamma-\tilde{\gamma})}{s}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{\gamma}t^{-\frac{n+s}{s}}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\tau^{-\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}d\tau\,\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{\gamma}t^{-\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\,\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}\Big).

For
\mathcal{I}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{\tilde{\gamma}}t^{-\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\min\left\{1,\frac{|\alpha|}{\tau^{\frac{1}{s}}}\right\}^{1-\tilde{\gamma}}\tau^{-\frac{s-(\gamma-\tilde{\gamma})}{s}}d\tau\ \Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\tilde{\sigma}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{\gamma}t^{-\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\tilde{\sigma}}}\Big).

Then we obtain

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{\tilde{n}}\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t,x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{\gamma}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(t^{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}+t^{\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\tilde{\sigma}}}).

Hence, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}}[h](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\sigma_{3}+\sigma_{4}}}\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(t^{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\tilde{\sigma}}}+t^{\frac{n+\sigma_{3}}{s}}\|h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\tilde{\sigma}}}\right).

Combining this with (
2.25
) yields (
2.23
).
This completes the proof of the lemma.

By Lemma
2.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|u_{L,x_{0}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{b}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u_{L,x_{0}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{b+m+\kappa}})\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{b}},

(2.26)

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(t^{\frac{\eta}{s}}\|u_{L,x_{0}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{b+\eta}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u_{L,x_{0}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{b+m+\kappa}})\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{b}},

and

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|u_{N,x_{0}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{b}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u_{N,x_{0}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{b+m+\kappa}})\lesssim\mathsf{Data}_{T}(f).

(2.27)

It remains to estimate

u_{R,{x_{0}}}(t,x)=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)R_{x_{0}}[u](\tau,z)dzd\tau,

where
R_{x_{0}}[u]
is defined in (
2.3
).
By Lemma
6.6
and (
1.10
), for any
x_{0},x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle|R_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)|\lesssim t^{-\frac{\eta}{s}}|x_{0}-x|^{\eta}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{\dot{C}^{s-\varepsilon}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|_{\dot{C}^{s+\varepsilon}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim|x_{0}-x|^{\eta}t^{-\frac{\eta+s-b}{s}}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},

(2.28)

for any
s-\varepsilon\geq b+\eta
.
For the case
b\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, by (
2.28
) and Corollary
2.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\nabla^{b}u_{R,x_{0}}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla^{b}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||R_{x_{0}}[u](\tau,z)|dzd\tau\Big|_{x_{0}=x}

(2.29)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\tau^{-\frac{\eta+s-b}{s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla^{b}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||x-z|^{\eta}dzd\tau\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\tau^{-\frac{\eta+s-b}{s}}\tau^{-\frac{b-\eta}{s}}d\tau\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}\lesssim\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}.

If
b\notin\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{[b]}u_{R,x_{0}}|_{x_{0}=x}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{[b]}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||R_{x_{0}}[u](\tau,z)|dzd\tau\Big|_{x_{0}=x}

(2.30)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\tau^{-\frac{\eta+s-b}{s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{[b]}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||x-z|^{\eta}dzd\tau\,\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\,\|u\|_{T,*}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{b-[b]}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},

provided
\eta\leq b-[b]
.

To estimate higher-order derivatives, we split the time integral in
u_{R,x_{0}}
over the intervals
[0,\frac{t}{2}]
and
[\frac{t}{2},t]
, denoting the resulting contributions as
u_{R,x_{0}}^{1}
and
u_{R,x_{0}}^{2}
, respectively. For
u_{R,x_{0}}^{1}
, by a similar proof as (
2.30
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]}u_{R,x_{0}}^{1}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)R_{x_{0}}[u](\tau,z)dzd\tau\Big|_{x_{0}=x}\right|

(2.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\alpha|^{\kappa+b-[\kappa+b]}t^{-\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},

provided
\eta\leq\kappa+b-[\kappa+b]
.

To estimate
u_{R,x_{0}}^{2}
, we need to deal with higher order derivative of
R_{x_{0}}[u]
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\sigma\in(0,1)
such that
n+\sigma\leq b+m+\kappa-s
.
Using the inequality

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}(fg)(x)-f(x)\delta_{\alpha}g(x)|\leq\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\delta_{-\alpha}f\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\delta_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}},

we obtain

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{n}R_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)-R_{x_{0}}[\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{n}u](t,x)|\lesssim\min\{(|\alpha|t^{-\frac{1}{s}})^{\eta},|\alpha|t^{-\frac{1}{s}}\}t^{-\frac{n+s-b}{s}}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}.

Combining this with (
2.28
) yields

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{n}R_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)|\lesssim

\displaystyle|R_{x_{0}}[\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{n}u](t,x)|+\min\{(|\alpha|t^{-\frac{1}{s}})^{\eta},|\alpha|t^{-\frac{1}{s}}\}t^{-\frac{n+s-b}{s}}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(|\alpha|^{\sigma}|x-x_{0}|^{\eta}t^{-\frac{\eta+\sigma}{s}}+\min\{(|\alpha|t^{-\frac{1}{s}})^{\eta},|\alpha|t^{-\frac{1}{s}}\})t^{-\frac{n+s-b}{s}}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}.

The first term vanishes if we take
x_{0}=x
. This indicates that the derivatives are not carried solely by
u
, part of them are absorbed by the coefficient
\mathsf{A}
. This mechanism is essential for avoiding the time singularity as
\tau
approaches
t
. Furthermore, for double finite difference operator
\delta_{\alpha}\delta_{\beta}
, and any
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in[0,1]
with
n+\max\{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\}\leq b+m+\kappa-s
and
\min\{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\}\geq\eta
, it holds

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\delta_{\beta}\nabla^{n}R_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)||_{x_{0}=x}\lesssim t^{-\frac{n+s-b+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}{s}}|\alpha|^{\mu_{1}}|\beta|^{\mu_{2}}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}.

Denote
\bar{s}=s\mathbf{1}_{s\in\mathbb{N}}+([s]+1)\mathbf{1}_{s\notin\mathbb{N}}
. From this, we obtain

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]-\bar{s}}R_{x_{0}}(t,z)-\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]-\bar{s}}R_{x_{0}}(t,x)|\Big|_{x_{0}=x}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\lesssim t^{-\frac{m+\kappa+s-\bar{s}+\tilde{\eta}}{s}}|\alpha|^{\kappa+b-[\kappa+b]}|x-z|^{\tilde{\eta}}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},

where
\tilde{\eta}=\eta\mathbf{1}_{s\in\mathbb{N}}+\min\{(\kappa+b-s-[\kappa+b]+\bar{s}),1\}\mathbf{1}_{s\notin\mathbb{N}}
.
Using integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]}u_{R,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}|

(2.32)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla^{\bar{s}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]-\bar{s}}R_{x_{0}}(\tau,z)-\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]-\bar{s}}R_{x_{0}}(\tau,x)|dzd\tau\Big|_{x_{0}=x}

\displaystyle\lesssim[\alpha]^{\kappa+b-[\kappa+b]}t^{-\frac{m+\kappa+s-\bar{s}+\tilde{\eta}}{s}}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla^{\bar{s}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,z)||z|^{\tilde{\eta}}dzd\tau\Big|_{x_{0}=x}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}.

From Corollary
2.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\nabla^{\bar{s}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,z)||z|^{\tilde{\eta}}dz\lesssim(t-\tau)^{-\frac{\bar{s}-\tilde{\eta}}{s}}.

We have
\bar{s}-\tilde{\eta}<s
.
Integrating in time and
combining with (
2.32
), we deduce that

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]}u_{R,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}|\lesssim|\alpha|^{\kappa+b-[\kappa+b]}t^{-\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}.

(2.33)

Combining (
2.29
), (
2.30
), (
2.31
) and (
2.33
), we get

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|\nabla^{b}u_{R,x_{0}}(t)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]}u_{R,x_{0}}(t)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa+b-[\kappa+b]}}\right)\leq C\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},\quad b\in\mathbb{N}_{+},

(2.34)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\frac{\|\nabla^{[b]}u_{R,x_{0}}(t)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{b-[b]}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa+b]}u_{R,x_{0}}(t)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa+b-[\kappa+b]}}\right)\leq C\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},\quad b\notin\mathbb{N}_{+}.

We conclude from (
2.20
), (
2.26
), (
2.27
) and (
2.34
) that

\|u\|_{T}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{b}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(f)+\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},

and the non-endpoint result

\|u\|_{T,*}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{b}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(f)+\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}.

This completes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Corollary 2.6

In the same setting as Theorem
1.1
, the proof of Theorem
1.1
also yields the following estimates:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|\Lambda^{b}u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|\Lambda^{b}u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\lesssim\|\Lambda^{b}u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(f)+\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},

(2.35)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(t^{\frac{\eta}{s}}\|\Lambda^{b}u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\eta}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|\Lambda^{b}u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{b}_{\infty,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(f)+\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*}.

(2.36)

Remark 2.7

The implicit constants in (
1.13
) and (
2.35
) can be controlled by
\frac{(c_{0}^{-1}c_{1})^{m+s+2}}{\kappa_{0}(s-\kappa)}
, the implicit constants in (
1.14
) and (
2.36
) can be controlled by
\frac{(c_{0}^{-1}c_{1})^{m+s+2}}{\eta\kappa_{0}(s-\kappa)}
,. Furthermore, when only
\|u\|_{\dot{C}^{b}}
or
\|\Lambda^{b}u\|_{L^{\infty}}
is considered (i.e., without higher regularity term), the estimates

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{b}}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{b}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(f)+\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\Lambda^{b}u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{b}u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(f)+\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|u\|_{T,*},

hold with implicit constants independent of
c_{0},c_{1}
, see (
2.18
). This is natural since no smoothing effect is involved in this case, and the bound does not rely on the lower or upper bound of the coefficient
\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)
.

Remark 2.8

In contrast to conventional approaches in existing literature, our methodology avoids the introduction of spatial truncations during coefficient freezing. Instead, we fix the spatial coordinate
x_{0}

in the frozen coefficients and explicitly construct the fundamental solution (Green’s function) of the linearized equation. This enables us to derive a closed-form representation formula for the solution. By differentiating this formula and subsequently setting

x_{0}=x
, we rigorously demonstrate that the coefficient freezing procedure only generates residual terms of lower differential order. This approach eliminates artificial truncation errors while preserving the intrinsic regularity structure of the problem.

Finally, as an extension to Theorem
1.1
, we establish the following proposition for the endpoint case
b=0
, provided that the coefficient
\mathsf{A}
is regular in
x
and the forcing term possesses a derivative structure. Specifically, consider the system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u(t,x)+\mathcal{L}_{s}u(t,x)=\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}f(t,x)+g(t,x),\ \ \ (t,x)\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},

(2.37)

\displaystyle u|_{t=0}=u_{0},

where
\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}
is a differential operator defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}f(t,x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{B}(\xi)\hat{f}(t,\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}d\xi,\ \ \ \ 0<\gamma\leq s,

(2.38)

where the symbol
\mathsf{B}(\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
satisfies the growth condition

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{j}_{\xi}\mathsf{B}(\xi)\right|\lesssim|\xi|^{\gamma-j},\ \ \ \forall 0\leq j\leq d+\kappa+2.

The operator
\mathcal{L}_{s}
is defined by (
1.8
) with
\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)
satisfying (
1.9
). Moreover, suppose that there exists
\mathsf{M}>1
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\leq m+\kappa+2\\
l\leq d+s+2\end{subarray}}{|\xi|^{l-s}}{\left|\nabla_{x}^{j}\nabla^{l}_{\xi}\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)\right|}\leq\mathsf{M},\ \ \forall\xi\neq 0,(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(2.39)

We define the following quantity for the force terms,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(t^{\frac{\kappa}{s}}\|f(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa_{\gamma}}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|f(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa_{\gamma}}}\right)+\|g\|_{L_{T}^{1}L^{\infty}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t^{\frac{m}{s}+1}\|g(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m}},

with
\kappa_{\gamma}=\kappa+\gamma-s
. We have the following Schauder-type estimate.

Proposition 2.9

For any
T>0
, consider the Cauchy problem (
2.37
) with
\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)
satisfying (
1.9
) and (
2.39
), if
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)<\infty
, then there exists a unique solution
u\in C((0,T],L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap L^{\infty}_{loc}((0,T],C^{m}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, and the following estimates hold:

i)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\lesssim e^{CT\log(T+2)}\left(\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)\right),

(2.40)

where
C>0
is a constant depending only on
\mathsf{M},m,s,c_{0}
.

ii) If
\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)
is independent of
x
, we have the improved estimate:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g).

(2.41)

Remark 2.10

i) The implicit constants in (
2.40
) and (
2.41
) can be controlled by
\frac{(c_{0}^{-1}c_{1})^{m+s+2}}{\kappa_{\gamma}(s-\kappa)}
. Furthermore, when only the
L^{\infty}
norm is considered (i.e., without the Hölder regularity term), the estimate

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim e^{CT\log(T+2)}\Big(\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)\Big)

holds with implicit constants independent of
c_{0},c_{1}
, see (
2.18
).

ii) The estimate (
2.40
) can be applied to obtain local well-posedness of some quasilinear equations with initial data satisfying some “continuity condition” (which indicates that the variable coefficient can be approximated by smooth function). Moreover, if
\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)
is independent of
x
, the estimate (
2.41
) holds, where the right-hand side does not have an exponential growth in time. This will be applied to obtain global well-posedness of some quasilinear equations with small initial data (which indicates that the variable coefficient can be approximated by a constant).

iii) The estimates in Proposition
2.9
are optimal,
being scaling-invariant with respect to (
2.37
).

iv) We can extend the estimates in Proposition
2.9
to ultra-parabolic equations, where the operator
\mathcal{L}_{s}
is hypoelliptic and the system (
2.37
) also exhibits smoothing effect (see
[
66
,
101
,
102
]
).

Proof.
The proof of Proposition
2.9
consists of the following two main parts. First we establish a priori estimate for (
2.37
), where uniqueness follows, then we prove the existence of solution via compactness method.

Step 1: A priori estimate.

Assume
u
is a smooth solution to (
2.37
).
Denote

\|u\|_{X_{T}}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}).

We recall the decomposition of the solution
u=u_{L,x_{0}}+u_{N,x_{0}}+u_{R,x_{0}}
as in (
2.5
), where
u_{N,x_{0}}=u_{N,x_{0}}^{1}+u_{N,x_{0}}^{2}
, with

\displaystyle u_{N,x_{0}}^{1}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)\,\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}f(\tau,z)\,dzd\tau,

\displaystyle u_{N,x_{0}}^{2}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)\,g(\tau,z)\,dzd\tau.

By Lemma
2.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|u_{L,x_{0}}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{\alpha}t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{L,x_{0}}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},

(2.42)

\displaystyle\|u_{N,x_{0}}^{1}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{\alpha}t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{N,x_{0}}^{1}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}}\lesssim\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,0).

On the other hand, for
u_{N,x_{0}}^{2}
, by (
2.17
), it is easy to check that

\displaystyle|u_{N,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau)\|_{L^{1}}\|g(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}}.

For Hölder norm, by (
2.16
) we have

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{N,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)\right||g(\tau,z)|dzd\tau+\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{[\kappa]}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)\right|\left|\nabla^{m}g(\tau,z)\right|dzd\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\frac{1}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{m+[\kappa]}{s}}}\min\left\{1,\frac{|\alpha|}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{1}{s}}}\right\}\|g(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau+\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\frac{1}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{[\kappa]}{s}}}\min\left\{1,\frac{|\alpha|}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{1}{s}}}\right\}\|g(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{m}}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}t^{-\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\left(\|g\|_{L^{1}_{T}L^{\infty}}+\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[0,T]}\tau^{\prime\frac{m}{s}+1}\|g(\tau^{\prime})\|_{\dot{C}^{m}}\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\|u_{N,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{\alpha}t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{N,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}}\lesssim\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(0,g).

(2.43)

If
\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)=\mathsf{A}(t,\xi)
, then
u_{R,x_{0}}\equiv 0
. The estimate (
2.41
) follows from (
2.42
) and (
2.43
).

In the case where
\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)
varies with
x
, it remains to consider
u_{R,x_{0}}
. Recall
R_{x_{0}}[u]
defined in (
2.3
).
Let
0<\sigma<\min\{1,s\}
.
Note that

\displaystyle R_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)=\Lambda^{\sigma}R^{1}_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)+R^{2}[u](t,x),

(2.44)

where

\displaystyle R^{1}_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\xi|^{-\sigma}\bigl(\mathsf{A}(t,x_{0},\xi)-\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)\bigr)\widehat{u}(t,\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}\,d\xi,

\displaystyle R^{2}[u](t,x)

\displaystyle=C_{d,\sigma}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\xi|^{-\sigma}\bigl(\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)-\mathsf{A}(t,x-z,\xi)\bigr)\widehat{\tau_{z}u}(t,\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}\,\frac{d\xi\,dz}{|z|^{d+\sigma}},

with
\tau_{z}u(x)=u(x-z)
.
We briefly justify this decomposition. For a function

h(x,y):\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, denote by

\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
the fractional Laplacian acting on the

x
- and
y
-variables, respectively. Let

h_{1}:=\Lambda_{1}^{-\sigma}h.

Then

h=\Lambda_{1}^{\sigma}h_{1}.

Using the identity

h_{1}(x,x)-h_{1}(x-z,x)=\bigl(h_{1}(x,x)-h_{1}(x-z,x-z)\bigr)+\bigl(h_{1}(x-z,x-z)-h_{1}(x-z,x)\bigr),

we obtain

\displaystyle h(x,x)=\Lambda_{1}^{\sigma}h_{1}(\cdot,x)(x)=\Lambda_{x}^{\sigma}\bigl(h_{1}(\cdot,\cdot)\bigr)(x)+h_{R}(x),

(2.45)

where the commutator term
h_{R}
is given by

\displaystyle h_{R}(x)

\displaystyle:=\Lambda_{1}^{\sigma}h_{1}(\cdot,x)(x)-\Lambda_{x}^{\sigma}\bigl(h_{1}(\cdot,\cdot)\bigr)(x)\bigr)

\displaystyle=C_{d,\sigma}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigl(h_{1}(x-z,x-z)-h_{1}(x-z,x)\bigr)\frac{dz}{|z|^{d+\sigma}}.

Now take

h(x,y)=(2\pi)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigl(\mathsf{A}(t,x_{0},\xi)-\mathsf{A}(t,y,\xi)\bigr)\widehat{u}(t,\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}\,d\xi.

Then

R_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)=h(x,x),\qquad R^{1}_{x_{0}}[u](t,x)=h_{1}(x,x),\qquad R^{2}[u](t,x)=h_{R}(x),

and thus (
2.44
) follows from (
2.45
).

From (
2.44
) and (
2.5
), we have

\displaystyle u_{R,{x_{0}}}(t,x)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Lambda^{\sigma}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)R_{x_{0}}^{1}[u](\tau,z)dzd\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)R^{2}[u](\tau,z)dzd\tau

\displaystyle:=u_{R,{x_{0}}}^{1}(t,x)+u_{R,{x_{0}}}^{2}(t,x).

Following (
2.43
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|u_{R,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}+

\displaystyle\sup_{\alpha}t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{R,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}}\lesssim\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(0,R^{2}[u]).

By Lemma
6.6
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}_{x}R^{2}[u](t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\mathsf{M}t^{-\frac{k+s-\sigma}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}},\ \ \ \forall\ 0\leq k\leq m.

This implies
\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(0,R^{2}[u])\lesssim\mathsf{M}T^{\frac{\sigma}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}}
. Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\|u_{R,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}+

\displaystyle\sup_{\alpha}t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{R,x_{0}}^{2}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}}\lesssim\mathsf{M}T^{\frac{\sigma}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}}.

(2.46)

Then we estimate
u_{R,x_{0}}^{1}
. By the Lemma
6.6
, we have for any
\eta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle|R_{x_{0}}^{1}[u](t,x)|\lesssim\mathsf{M}|x_{0}-x|^{\eta^{\prime}}\|u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{s-\sigma-\varepsilon}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{s-\sigma+\varepsilon}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\mathsf{M}|x_{0}-x|^{\eta^{\prime}}t^{-\frac{s-\sigma}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}},

(2.47)

for some
\varepsilon
small enough. Similarly, for any
0\leq k\leq m
and
\eta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\nabla^{k}_{x}R_{x_{0}}^{1}[u](t,x)|\lesssim\mathsf{M}(|x_{0}-x|^{\eta^{\prime}}+t^{\frac{1}{s}})t^{-\frac{k+s-\sigma}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}}.

By (
2.47
) an Lemma
2.1
,

\displaystyle|u_{R,x_{0}}^{1}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathsf{M}\int_{0}^{t}\tau^{-\frac{s-\sigma}{s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\Lambda^{\sigma}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)||x-z|^{\eta^{\prime}}dzd\tau\|u\|_{X_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathsf{M}\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{\sigma-\eta^{\prime}}{s}}\tau^{-\frac{s-\sigma}{s}}d\tau\|u\|_{X_{T}}\lesssim\mathsf{M}T^{\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}},

for any
0<\eta^{\prime}<\sigma
.
Then we estimate higher order derivatives.
By taking
\sigma\in(s-\kappa,s-[\kappa])
, and decomposing the integral interval into
[0,\frac{t}{2}]\cup[\frac{t}{2},t]
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{R,x_{0}}^{1}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}\Lambda_{z}^{\sigma}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)R_{x_{0}}^{1}(\tau,z)dzd\tau\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{[\kappa]}\Lambda_{z}^{\sigma}\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,\tau,x-z)\nabla^{m}R_{x_{0}}^{1}(\tau,z)dzd\tau\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}+|x_{0}-x|^{\kappa-[\kappa]})T^{\frac{\kappa-[\kappa]}{s}}t^{-\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}}.

So we have proved

\displaystyle\|u_{R,x_{0}}^{1}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}+

\displaystyle\sup_{\alpha}t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{R,x_{0}}^{1}(t,x)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}}\lesssim\mathsf{M}T^{\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}}.

Combining this with (
2.46
) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|u_{R,x_{0}}(t)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\sup_{\alpha}\frac{\|\left(\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u_{R,x_{0}}(t)\right)|_{x_{0}=x}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}}\right)\leq C\mathsf{M}T^{\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T}},

provided
0<\eta^{\prime}<\sigma<\kappa-[\kappa]
. By taking
T
small enough such that
C\mathsf{M}T^{\frac{\sigma}{s}}<\frac{1}{100}
, from (
2.20
), (
2.42
) and (
2.43
)
we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\sup_{\alpha}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\nabla^{m+[\kappa]}u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa-[\kappa]}}\right)\lesssim\mathsf{M}\left(\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)\right).

(2.48)

From (
2.48
), if we start from
\frac{T_{0}}{2}
, it holds

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[\frac{T_{0}}{2},\frac{3T_{0}}{2}]}(\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+(t-T_{0}/2)^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\leq

\displaystyle C\mathsf{M}\left(\|u(T_{0}/2)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{3T_{0}/2}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)\right).

In particular, for
t\in[T_{0},3T_{0}/2]
, one has
t-T_{0}/2\sim t
, then we obtain

\displaystyle\|u\|_{X_{3T_{0}/2}}\leq C\mathsf{M}\left(\|u(T_{0}/2)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{3T_{0}/2}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)\right)\leq C^{2}\mathsf{M}^{2}(\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}_{3T_{0}/2}(f,g)).

Repeating the procedure
n-1
times, it follows that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{X_{(n+1)T_{0}/2}}\leq C^{n}\mathsf{M}^{n}(n!)^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}(\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}_{(n+1)T_{0}/2}(f,g)).

Take
n=[\frac{2T}{T_{0}}]+1
, then by the Stirling formula, we derive

\displaystyle\|u\|_{X_{T}}\lesssim e^{\tilde{C}T\log(T+2)}(\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)),\ \ \ \forall T>0,

which implies (
2.40
). This a priori estimate also ensures uniqueness by taking
u_{0}=f=g=0
.

Step 2: Existence of solution.

To prove the existence, we use the compactness method. Consider a regularized approximation system, for which the Cauchy problem is easily studied, and whose solutions are expected to converge to solutions of the original system (
2.37
). More precisely, let
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
, we consider for
u:(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u(t,x)+\mathcal{L}_{s}u(t,x)=(1-\chi_{\varepsilon_{1}}(t))(\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}f(t,x)+g(t,x))\quad\text{in}\ (0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},

\displaystyle u|_{t=0}=u_{0}\ast\rho_{\varepsilon_{2}}.

Here
\rho_{\varepsilon_{2}}
is the standard mollifier in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\chi_{\varepsilon_{1}}
is a smooth temporal cutoff function that satisfies
\mathbf{1}_{[0,\varepsilon_{1}]}\leq\chi_{\varepsilon_{1}}\leq\mathbf{1}_{[0,2\varepsilon_{1}]}
and the force term is supported away from time
0
and belongs to
C_{c}^{\infty}((0,\infty);C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. By the classical theory of parabolic equations, there exists a unique classical solution
u_{\varepsilon}=u_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}\in C([0,\infty),L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap L^{\infty}_{loc}((0,\infty),C^{m+\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, see Remark
2.11
. Moreover, for any
T>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|u_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\leq Ce^{\tilde{C}T\log(T+2)}(\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)).

(2.49)

By interpolation inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t\|\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}t(\|\mathcal{L}_{s}u_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}f(t)+g(t)\|_{L^{\infty}})

(2.50)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{\tilde{C}T\log(T+2)}(\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}^{m,\kappa,\gamma}(f,g)).

The estimates (
2.49
) and (
2.50
) guarantee the compactness and convergence of the sequence
\{u_{\varepsilon}\}
.
Taking
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\to 0
, we obtain that
u_{\varepsilon}
converges to
u\in C((0,T],L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap L^{\infty}_{loc}((0,T],C^{m+\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
for any
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, which is a solution to the original system (
2.37
) and satisfies the estimate (
2.49
). This completes the proof of the theorem.

We now prove Proposition
1.4
, which develops a general framework for deriving a priori estimates by applying Theorem
1.1
and Proposition
2.9
to generic quasilinear systems. This methodology is subsequently applied to several distinct model equations in Sections
3
–
5
.

Proof of Proposition
1.4
.
Recalling the norms
\|\cdot\|_{T},\|\cdot\|_{T,*}
defined in (
1.16
), we first give a priori estimates of
\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}
and
\|U-\Phi\|_{T,*}
.

Step 1: Estimate of
\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}
.

Here we assume
U(t,x)
is a regular solution. From (
1.15
), we have

\partial_{t}U(t,x)+A(\nabla\Phi(x),\nabla)U(t,x)=\mathcal{N}(U)(t,x)+\mathcal{R}(U,\Phi)(t,x),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(U,\Phi)(t,x)=A(\nabla\Phi(x),\nabla)U(t,x)-A(\nabla U(t,x),\nabla)U(t,x).

Applying Theorem
1.1
and (
1.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla U_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathcal{N}(U)+\mathcal{R}(U,\Phi))+\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|U\|_{T,*}

(2.51)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla U_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|U\|_{T,*}^{1+\epsilon_{0}}(1+\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|U\|_{T,*})^{c_{1}}

\displaystyle+(1+\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{x}})^{c_{2}}(\|U-\Phi\|_{T}\|U\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{s}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+2}}\|U\|_{T,*}).

Step 2: Estimate of
\|U-\Phi\|_{T,*}
.

Rewrite (
1.15
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(U-\Phi)+A(\nabla U,\nabla)(U-\Phi)=\mathcal{N}(U)-A(\nabla U,\nabla)\Phi.

By Theorem
1.1
and the estimates (
1.17
), (
1.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle||U-\Phi||_{T,*}

(2.52)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{x}})^{c_{2}(m+s)}\left(\|U_{0}-\Phi\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathcal{N}(U)-A(\nabla U,\nabla)\Phi)+\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\|U-\Phi\|_{T,*}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+||\nabla U||_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{x}}+||U||_{T,*})^{\frac{c}{2}}(\|U_{0}-\Phi\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}+||U||_{T,*}^{1+\epsilon_{0}}+T^{\frac{1}{s}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+s}}),

with
c=2(c_{1}+c_{2}(m+s)+c_{3}+1)
.
Now we denote

\displaystyle B(T):=\|U-\Phi\|_{T,*},\ \ \ \ M(T):=\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Note that

\displaystyle\|U\|_{T,*}\leq\|U-\Phi\|_{T,*}+\|\Phi\|_{T,*}\lesssim B(T)+T^{\frac{\eta}{s}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+s+1}}.

Hence, we write (
2.51
) and (
2.52
) as

\displaystyle M(T)\lesssim\|\nabla U_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+(B(T)+T^{\frac{\eta}{s}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+s+1}})(1+M(T)+B(T)+T^{\frac{1}{s}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+s+1}})^{\frac{c}{2}},

\displaystyle B(T)\lesssim(\|U_{0}-\Phi\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}+B(T)^{1+\varepsilon_{0}}+T^{\frac{1}{s}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+s+1}})(1+M(T)+B(T)+T^{\frac{1}{s}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+s+1}})^{\frac{c}{2}}.

It is easy to check that under the assumption

(1+||\nabla U_{0}||_{L^{\infty}})^{c}\|\nabla(U_{0}-\Phi)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{0}}\leq\varepsilon\ll 1,

one can take

T_{0}=T_{0}(\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+s+1}})
small enough such that

\displaystyle M(T_{0})\leq C(\varepsilon+\|\nabla U_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}),

\displaystyle B(T_{0})\leq C\varepsilon.

Moreover, if
\Phi\equiv 0
, then

\displaystyle M(T)\lesssim\|\nabla U_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+B(T)(1+M(T)+B(T))^{\frac{c}{2}},

\displaystyle B(T)\lesssim(\|U_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}+B(T)^{1+\varepsilon_{0}})(1+M(T)+B(T))^{\frac{c}{2}},\quad\quad\quad\forall T>0.

By the bootstrap method, we obtain

\displaystyle M(T)\leq C(\varepsilon+\|\nabla U_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}),

\displaystyle B(T)\leq C\varepsilon,\ \ \ \forall\ T>0.

This completes the proof of Proposition
1.4
.

2.3
Schauder type estimates for equations on manifolds

The extension of Theorem
1.1
to manifolds without boundary can be established as follows. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a smooth, closed manifold equipped with:

•

A finite atlas
\{\mathcal{U}_{i},\phi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
satisfying
\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{i}\Subset\mathcal{U}_{i}
for each
i
, where
\{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
forms an open cover of
\mathcal{M}
.

•

Local homeomorphisms
\phi_{i}:\mathcal{U}_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

•

A partition of unity
\{\chi_{i}\}
subordinate to
\mathcal{U}_{i}
with
\text{Supp}(\chi_{i})\subset\mathcal{U}_{i}
,
\chi_{i}|_{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{i}}=1
.

Define the Hölder norm on
\mathcal{M}
through localization:

\|u\|_{C^{m}(\mathcal{M})}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|(u\chi_{i})\circ\phi_{i}^{-1}\|_{C^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(2.53)

We denote
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{M}}^{s}
a Pseudo-differential operator on the manifold
\mathcal{M}
, which is defined via local coordinates:

(\chi_{i}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{M}}^{s}u)\circ\phi_{i}^{-1}=\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{s,i}((\chi_{i}u)\circ\phi_{i}^{-1})+R_{i}(u),

(2.54)

where
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{s,i}
,
i=1,\cdots,n
are Pseudo-differential operators in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as defined in (
1.8
) and (
1.9
), and the remainder term
R_{i}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|R_{i}(u)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim C_{\mathcal{M}}\|u\|_{C^{s-\zeta_{0}}(\mathcal{M})},\quad\|R_{i}(u)\|_{\dot{C}^{k+\alpha}}\lesssim C_{\mathcal{M}}\|u\|_{C^{s+k+\alpha-\zeta_{0}}(\mathcal{M})},\quad\forall k\leq m,\alpha\in[0,1),

(2.55)

for some
\zeta_{0}>0
.
Suppose that

(\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{M}}^{s}u,u)\geq c_{0}\|u\|_{H^{s_{0}}(\mathcal{M})}^{2}-c_{0}^{-1}\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M})}^{2},\quad\forall u\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}),

(2.56)

for some
s_{0}\in(0,\frac{s}{2}]
and
c_{0}\in(0,1).
Here
(\cdot,\cdot)
is the inner product on
\mathcal{M}
.

Consider the following system on
\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u(t,x)+\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{M}}^{s}u(t,x)=G(t,x),\quad\text{in}\ (0,\infty)\times\mathcal{M},

(2.57)

\displaystyle u|_{t=0}=u_{0},

with a known force term
G
.
The existence and uniqueness of solutions to (
2.57
) follow from standard parabolic theory combined with the Gårding’s inequality (
2.56
), adapting the techniques in
[
94
]
. See also
[
88
,
73
,
63
]
.

Remark 2.11

We discuss an iterative method to solve (
2.57
). The solution is given by

\displaystyle u(t)=S(t,0)u_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}S(t,\tau)G(\tau)d\tau,

(2.58)

where

S(t,\tau)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Pi_{k=1}^{n}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\frac{t-\tau}{n}\mathcal{L}^{s}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\tau+\frac{k(t-\tau)}{n}\right)\right)^{-1}.

To see this, we divide the time interval
[0,T]
into
n
parts, and denote
\Delta t=\frac{t-\tau}{n}
,
t_{j}=j\Delta t
, then consider the approximate system

\displaystyle\frac{u_{j+1}-u_{j}}{\Delta t}+\mathcal{L}^{s}_{\mathcal{M}}(t_{j})u_{j+1}=G_{j},\quad j=0,1,\cdots,n-1,

which is equivalent to

\frac{u_{j+1}}{\Delta t}+\mathcal{L}^{s}_{\mathcal{M}}(t_{j})u_{j+1}=\frac{u_{j}}{\Delta t}+G_{j},\quad j=0,1,\cdots,n-1.

Using
\frac{1}{\Delta t}\gg 1
and the Gårding inequality (
2.56
), we can solve inductively

\displaystyle u_{j+1}=S_{j}u_{j}+\Delta tS_{j}(G_{j}),

where

\displaystyle S_{j}=\left(\mathrm{Id}+\Delta t\mathcal{L}^{s}_{\mathcal{M}}(t_{j})\right)^{-1}.

This implies that

\displaystyle u_{n}=\prod_{j=1}^{n}S_{j}u_{0}+\Delta t\sum_{j=0}^{n}\prod_{k=j}^{n}S_{k}(G_{j}).

Taking
n\to\infty
yields the formula (
2.58
).

We prove the following a priori estimate for the solution to the evolution system (
2.57
), establishing a counterpart of Theorem
1.1
on the manifold.

Theorem 2.12

Let
0<T<\infty
. Suppose that
u\in C([0,T],L^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}))\cap L_{loc}^{\infty}((0,T],C^{m+\kappa}(\mathcal{M}))
is a solution to (
2.57
) with
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{M}}^{s}
satisfying (
2.54
).
If
u_{0}\in C^{n}(\mathcal{M})
with
s-\kappa\leq n\leq s
, and
R_{i}
satisfies (
2.55
) with
k\leq m+n
,
then

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|u(t)\|_{C^{n}(\mathcal{M})}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|u(t)\|_{C^{m+n+\kappa}(\mathcal{M})})\lesssim e^{CT\log(T+2)}(\|u_{0}\|_{C^{n}}+\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathcal{M}}(G)),

(2.59)

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathcal{M}}(g):=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(t^{\frac{\kappa}{s}}\|g(t)\|_{C^{\kappa-s+n}(\mathcal{M})}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|g(t)\|_{C^{m+\kappa-s+n}(\mathcal{M})}\right).

Proof.
Denote
v_{j}=(u\chi_{j})\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}
. By (
2.54
), we write the equation of
v_{j}
as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v_{j}(t,x)+\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{s,j}v_{j}(t,x)=G_{j}(t,x)+R_{j}(t,x),\quad\text{in}\ (0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d},

\displaystyle v_{j}|_{t=0}=(u_{0}\chi_{j})\circ\phi_{j}^{-1},

where
G_{j}=(G\chi_{j})\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}
, and

\displaystyle R_{j}(t,x)=\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{s,j}(v_{j})-(\chi_{j}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{M}}^{s}u)\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}.

Denote
v_{j}^{n}:=\nabla^{n}_{x}v_{j}
, the equation of
v_{j}^{n}
can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v_{j}^{n}(t,x)+\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{s,j}v_{j}^{n}(t,x)=\nabla^{n}(G_{j}(t,x)+R_{j}(t,x))+R_{j}^{n},\quad\text{in}\ (0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle v_{j}^{n}|_{t=0}=\nabla^{n}((u_{0}\chi_{j})\circ\phi_{j}^{-1}),

where

R_{j}^{n}=\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{s,j}v_{j}^{n}(t,x)-\nabla^{n}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{s,j}v_{j}).

For simplicity, denote

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{T}}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|f(t)\|_{C^{m+\kappa}}\right),

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{T,n}}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|f(t)\|_{C^{n}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|f(t)\|_{C^{m+n+\kappa}}\right),

where we drop the domain of the function, which can be
\mathcal{M}
or
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, whenever it is clear from context.

Let
T_{0}\in(0,\min\{T,1\}]
. It follows from Proposition
2.9
that

\displaystyle\|v_{j}^{n}\|_{X_{T_{0}}}\lesssim\|v_{j}^{n}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T_{0},\mathcal{M}}(G_{j})+\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T_{0},\mathcal{M}}(R_{j})+\|R_{j}^{n}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{1}L^{\infty}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}t^{\frac{m}{s}+1}\|R_{j}^{n}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m}}.

Note that by definition (
2.53
),

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\|v_{j}^{n}\|_{X_{T}}\sim\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\|v_{j}\|_{X_{T,n}}\sim\|u\|_{X_{T,n}},\quad\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\|v_{j}^{n}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}\sim\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\|v_{j}(0)\|_{C^{n}}\sim\|u_{0}\|_{C^{n}(\mathcal{M})}.

By the facts that

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathcal{M}}(G_{j})\sim\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathcal{M}}(G),

and

\|R_{j}^{n}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{1}L^{\infty}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}t^{\frac{m}{s}+1}\|R_{j}^{n}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m}}+\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T_{0},\mathcal{M}}(R_{j})\lesssim T_{0}^{\frac{\zeta_{0}}{s}}\|v_{j}\|_{X_{T_{0},n}},

where we use (
2.55
) and the constants depends on the domain. We obtain

\|u\|_{X_{T_{0},n}}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{C^{n}(\mathcal{M})}+\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathcal{M}}(G)+T_{0}^{\frac{\zeta_{0}}{s}}\|u\|_{X_{T_{0},n}}.

By a similar argument in the proof of Proposition
2.9
, we deduce

\|u\|_{X_{T,n}}\lesssim e^{C^{\prime}T\log(T+2)}\left(\|u_{0}\|_{C^{n}(\mathcal{M})}+\mathsf{Data}^{n,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathcal{M}}(G)\right),\ \ \ \forall T>0.

This completes the proof of the theorem.

3
The 2D Muskat equation with surface tension

In this section, we apply Theorem
1.1
to study the 2D Muskat equation with surface tension, given by (
1.21
). To do this, we first reformulate the nonlinear term.

For simplicity, fix the constants
\mathbf{k}=2,\mu=1
and
\sigma^{\prime}=1
.
We start by noting that the following holds:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\mathrm{P.V.}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1+\partial_{x}f(x)\Delta_{\alpha}f(x)}{\langle\Delta_{\alpha}f(x)\rangle^{2}}\partial_{x}\left(\kappa(f)-\varrho_{0}f\right)(x-\alpha)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha}=-\frac{\Lambda^{3}f(x)}{\langle\partial_{x}f(x)\rangle^{3}}+\mathsf{N}[f](x),

where
\mathsf{N}[f]=\mathsf{N}_{1}[f]+\mathsf{N}_{2}[f]+\varrho_{0}\mathsf{N}_{3}[f]
, with the following definitions:

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{1}[f](x)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta_{\alpha}f(x)(\partial_{x}f(x)-\Delta_{\alpha}f(x))}{\left\langle\Delta_{\alpha}f(x)\right\rangle^{2}}\right)\partial_{x}\left(\frac{\partial_{x}^{2}f(x-\alpha)}{\left\langle\partial_{x}f(x-\alpha)\right\rangle^{3}}\right)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha},

(3.1)

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{2}[f](x)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}^{2}f(x-\alpha)\left(\frac{1}{\left\langle\partial_{x}f(x-\alpha)\right\rangle^{3}}-\frac{1}{\left\langle\partial_{x}f(x)\right\rangle^{3}}\right)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{3}[f](x)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{\Delta_{\alpha}f(x)(\partial_{x}f(x)-\Delta_{\alpha}f(x))}{\left\langle\Delta_{\alpha}f(x)\right\rangle^{2}}\right)\partial_{x}f(x-\alpha)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha}-\Lambda f.

Thus, the 2D Muskat equation with surface tension (
1.21
) can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f(x)+\frac{\Lambda^{3}f(x)}{\langle\partial_{x}f(x)\rangle^{3}}=\mathsf{N}[f](x),

(3.2)

\displaystyle f|_{t=0}=f_{0}.

The proof of Theorem
1.5
is organized in three stages:

1.

Relaxed version:
As a preliminary step, we establish Proposition
3.1
, in which
the Besov norms in (
1.24
) and (
1.25
) are replaced by Lipschitz norms
.

2.

Existence theory:

For initial data satisfying (
1.24
) or (
1.25
), we establish a priori estimates and construct solutions via compactness arguments.

3.

Stability analysis:

We derive continuous dependence on initial data for the obtained solutions, which yields the uniqueness of the solution.

We begin with the following relaxed version of Theorem
1.5
.

Proposition 3.1

There exist
\varepsilon_{0},C>0
such that the following statements hold.

i) (
\varrho_{0}=0
) For any initial data
f_{0}\in\dot{W}^{1,\infty}
satisfying the smallness condition

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq\varepsilon_{0},

(3.3)

the equation (
1.21
) admits a unique global solution
f
in the class

\{f\in L^{\infty}_{loc}((0,\infty),C^{m+\kappa}(\mathbb{R})):\|f\|_{\infty}\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}},\|f\|_{\infty,*}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}\},

with
\|\cdot\|_{T}
,
\|\cdot\|_{T,*}
defined in (
1.23
).

ii) (
\varrho_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
) For any initial data
f_{0}\in W^{1,\infty}
, if

\displaystyle(1+\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})^{2(m+5)}\|f_{0}-\phi\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq\varepsilon_{0},

(3.4)

holds for some
\phi\in C^{m+5}(\mathbb{R})
,
then there exists
T>0
such that (
1.21
) admits a unique solution
f
in the class

\{f\in L^{\infty}_{loc}((0,T),C^{m+\kappa}(\mathbb{R})):\|f\|_{X_{T}}\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}},\|f\|_{T,*}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}\}.

Remark 3.2

For conciseness, we term Theorem
1.5
the Besov-type result and Proposition
3.1
the Lipschitz-type result. Notably, the Lipschitz-type result holds independent ofterest for studying well-posedness in certain quasilinear parabolic equations where the Besov-type approach is inapplicable due to structural constraints in nonlinear terms.

Now we start the proof of Proposition
3.1
which relies on the contraction mapping theorem.

Proof.
We first prove
i)
. Suppose
\varrho_{0}=0
and
\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
. Consider the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}=\left\{g\in L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}:g|_{t=0}=f_{0},\|g\|_{\infty}\leq\sigma\right\},

where
\sigma>0
is a constant that will be fixed later. For
g\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}
, define a map
\mathcal{S}g=f
, where
f
is the solution to the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f+{\Lambda^{3}f(x)}=\mathsf{N}[g](x)+G[g_{1},0],

\displaystyle f|_{t=0}=f_{0},

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}[g_{1},g_{2}]=\left(\frac{1}{\langle\partial_{x}g_{2}\rangle^{3}}-\frac{1}{\langle\partial_{x}g_{1}\rangle^{3}}\right)\Lambda^{3}g_{1}.

(3.5)

In the following, we prove that
\mathcal{S}
is a contraction map from
\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}
to itself. Therefore, there exists a unique fixed point in
\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}
, which is a global solution to (
1.21
).

First, we prove
\mathcal{S}:\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}
. Indeed, we apply Proposition
2.9
to obtain that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{\infty}(\mathsf{N}[g])+\mathsf{Data}_{\infty}(\mathsf{G}[g,0]).

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(F):=\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\left(t^{\frac{\kappa}{3}}\|F(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-2}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{3}}\|F(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa-2}}\right).

(3.6)

It follows from Lemma
6.10
that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{\infty}(\mathsf{N}[g])\lesssim\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+\|g\|_{\infty})^{2m+5}.

Applying Lemma
6.11
with
f_{1}=g
,
f_{2}\equiv 0
, and
\phi\equiv 0
gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{\infty}(\mathsf{G}[g,0])\lesssim\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+\|g\|_{\infty})^{2m+5}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C_{0}\|g\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+\|g\|_{\infty})^{2m+5},

where the constant
C_{0}
depends only on
m
. Take
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\frac{1}{100(2C_{0}+1)^{2(m+5)}}
and
\sigma=2C_{0}\varepsilon_{0}
. Then for any
g\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\varepsilon_{0}+4C_{0}^{3}\varepsilon_{0}^{2}(2C_{0}\varepsilon_{0}+1)^{2m+5}\leq\sigma,

provided (
3.3
) holds. This implies
f=\mathcal{S}g\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}
.

Next, we prove that
\|\mathcal{S}g_{1}-\mathcal{S}g_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\infty}
for
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}
with
\sigma
small enough.
Let
f_{1}=\mathcal{S}g_{1},f_{2}=\mathcal{S}g_{2}
. Then

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(f_{1}-f_{2})(x)+{\Lambda^{3}(f_{1}-f_{2})(x)}=(\mathsf{N}[g_{1}]-\mathsf{N}[g_{2}])(x)+(\mathsf{G}[g_{1},0]-\mathsf{G}[g_{2},0])(x),

\displaystyle(f_{1}-f_{2})|_{t=0}=0.

Applying Theorem
1.1
yields

\displaystyle\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{\infty}(\mathsf{N}[g_{1}]-\mathsf{N}[g_{2}])+\mathsf{Data}_{\infty}(\mathsf{G}[g_{1},0]-\mathsf{G}[g_{2},0]).

Lemma
6.10
and Lemma
6.11
imply

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{\infty}(\mathsf{N}[g_{1}]-\mathsf{N}[g_{2}])+\mathsf{Data}_{\infty}(\mathsf{G}[g_{1},0]-\mathsf{G}[g_{2},0])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\lesssim\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\infty}\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\infty}(1+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\infty})^{2m+5}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\infty}\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\infty}(1+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\infty})^{2m+5}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sigma(1+2\sigma)^{2m+5}\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\infty},

provided
C_{1}\sigma(1+2\sigma)^{2m+5}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
Thus, there exists a unique
f\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}
such that
\mathcal{S}f=f
. This completes the proof of
i)
.

Now we prove
ii)
. When
\varrho_{0}\neq 0
, the equation (
1.21
) is not homogeneous. We need to control the lower-order norm
\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}
.
For this, we define

\displaystyle\|h\|_{X_{T}}:=\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}+\|h\|_{T}.

Consider the set

\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma}=\{g\in L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}:g|_{t=0}=f_{0},\|g-\phi\|_{X_{T}}\leq\sigma\}.

For any
g\in\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma}
, where
T,\sigma
will be fixed later, define a map
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}g=f
, where
f
is the solution to the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f+\frac{\Lambda^{3}f}{\langle\partial_{x}\phi\rangle^{3}}=\mathsf{N}[g]+\mathsf{G}[g,\phi],

(3.7)

\displaystyle f|_{t=0}=f_{0},

where
\mathsf{G}[g,\phi]
is defined in (
3.5
).

In the following, we prove that
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
is a contraction map from
\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma}
to itself. Hence, there exists a unique fixed point in
\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma}
, which is a solution to (
1.21
). Indeed, denoting
\tilde{f}(t,x)=f(t,x)-\phi(x)
,
\tilde{g}(t,x)=g(t,x)-\phi(x)
, we obtain the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{f}+\frac{\Lambda^{3}\tilde{f}}{\langle\partial_{x}\phi\rangle^{3}}=\mathsf{N}[g]+\mathsf{G}[g,\phi]-\frac{\Lambda^{3}\phi}{\langle\partial_{x}\phi\rangle^{3}},

\displaystyle\tilde{f}|_{t=0}=f_{0}-\phi.

Applying Theorem
1.1
, we obtain for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}\|_{T}\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\|\partial_{x}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}})^{m+5}\left(\|\partial_{x}(f_{0}-\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{N}[g]+\mathsf{G}[g,\phi]-\frac{\Lambda^{3}\phi}{\langle\partial_{x}\phi\rangle^{3}})+\|\tilde{f}\|_{T}\|\phi\|_{T,*}\right),

(3.8)

where the additional factor
(1+\|\partial_{x}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}})^{m+5}
comes from the underlying lower bound of the coefficient
\frac{1}{\langle\partial_{x}\phi\rangle^{3}}
in (
3.2
), see Remark
2.7
.
Lemma
6.10
yields

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{N}[g])\lesssim(\|g\|_{T,*}^{2}+T^{\frac{2}{3}}\|g\|_{X_{T}})(1+\|g\|_{X_{T}})^{2m+5}.

(3.9)

Note that
\|\partial_{x}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}
, and

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{T,*}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{15}}\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}},\quad\quad\quad\|g\|_{T,*}\lesssim\|\tilde{g}\|_{X_{T}}+T^{\frac{1}{15}}\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}}.

(3.10)

Applying Lemma
6.11
with
f_{1}=g
,
f_{2}=\phi
yields

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{G}[g,\phi])\lesssim\|\tilde{g}\|_{T}(\|g\|_{T,*}+\|\tilde{g}\|_{T})

\displaystyle(1+\|(g,\phi)\|_{T})^{m+5}.

(3.11)

Finally, by the smoothness of
\phi
, it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{3}\phi}{\langle\partial_{x}\phi\rangle^{3}}\right)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{15}}\|\phi\|_{C^{m+4}}(1+\|\phi\|_{T})^{m+4}.

(3.12)

We conclude from (
3.8
), (
3.9
), (
3.11
) and (
3.12
) that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}\|_{T}\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{m+5}

(3.13)

\displaystyle\times\left(\|\partial_{x}(f_{0}-\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}}+(\|g\|_{T,*}^{2}+T^{\frac{1}{15}}\|\phi\|_{C^{m+3}}+\|\tilde{g}\|_{X_{T}}^{2})(1+\|(f,g,\phi)\|_{X_{T}})^{2(m+5)}\right).

On the other hand, integrating (
3.7
) in time, thanks to (
6.10
) and Remark
6.12
, we obtain

\displaystyle|f(t,x)-f_{0}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}(\|\Lambda^{3}f(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathsf{N}[g](\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathsf{G}[g,\phi](\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}})d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{0}t^{\frac{1}{3}}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}}+\|(g,\phi)\|_{X_{T}})^{5},\ \ \ \forall t\in[0,T].

From this we obtain

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}\leq\|f_{0}-\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}+T^{\frac{1}{3}}(1+\|f\|_{X_{T}}+\|(g,\phi)\|_{X_{T}})^{5}.

Combining this with (
3.13
) and (
3.10
),
we derive

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}\|_{X_{T}}\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{0}(1+\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{m+5}

\displaystyle\times\left(\|\partial_{x}(f_{0}-\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left(\|\tilde{g}\|_{X_{T}}+T^{\frac{1}{20}}(1+\|(\tilde{f},\tilde{g},\phi)\|_{X_{T}})\right)^{2}\left(1+\|(\tilde{f},\tilde{g},\phi)\|_{X_{T}}\right)^{2(m+5)}\right).

Here the constant
\tilde{C}_{0}
depends only on
m
and
\varrho_{0}
.
Let

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{0}\leq(10+\tilde{C}_{0})^{-10(m+d+5)},\quad\sigma^{\prime}=2\tilde{C}_{0}\varepsilon_{0},\quad\quad\text{and}\quad 0<T<\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{1+\tilde{C}_{0}+\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}}}\right)^{(m+10)^{2}}.

(3.14)

If (
3.4
) holds, then for any
g\in\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}\|_{X_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}+\tilde{C}_{0}(2\tilde{C}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}+T^{\frac{1}{20}})\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})^{2}(1+\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})^{3(m+5)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\tilde{C}_{0}\varepsilon_{0},

hence
f\in\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma^{\prime}}
.

In the following, we do the contraction estimates. Consider
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{T,\phi}
. Denote
\mathbf{g}=g_{1}-g_{2}
,
\mathbf{f}=f_{1}-f_{2}=\tilde{\mathcal{S}}g_{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{S}}g_{2}
. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{f}+\frac{\Lambda^{3}\mathbf{f}}{\langle\partial_{x}\phi\rangle^{3}}=\mathsf{N}[g_{1}]-\mathsf{N}[g_{2}]+\mathsf{G}[g_{1},\phi]-\mathsf{G}[g_{2},\phi],

(3.15)

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}|_{t=0}=0.

Applying Theorem
1.1
yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{T}\lesssim(1+\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{m+5}\left(\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{N}[g_{1}]-\mathsf{N}[g_{2}])+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{G}[g_{1},\phi]-\mathsf{G}[g_{2},\phi])\right).

(3.16)

From Lemma
6.10
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{N}[g_{1}]-\mathsf{N}[g_{2}])\lesssim(1+\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{m+5}\|\mathbf{g}\|_{X_{T}}(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{10}})(1+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{X_{T}})^{2(m+5)}.

Moreover, by Lemma
6.11
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{G}[g_{1},\phi]-\mathsf{G}[g_{2},\phi])\lesssim\|\mathbf{g}\|_{T}(\|g_{1}\|_{T,*}+\|g_{2}-\phi\|_{T})(1+\|(g_{1},g_{2},\phi)\|_{T})^{m+5}.

Combining this with (
3.16
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{T}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}\|_{X_{T}}(\|(g_{1}-\phi,g_{2}-\phi)\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{15}}\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}})(1+\|(g_{1},g_{2},\phi)\|_{X_{T}})^{2(m+5)}.

(3.17)

Finally, to control
\|\mathbf{f}\|_{X_{T}}
, it remains to estimate
\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}
. Integrating (
3.15
) in time, and applying (
6.10
) and Remark
6.12
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left(\|\Lambda^{3}\mathbf{f}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\mathsf{N}[g_{1}]-\mathsf{N}[g_{2}])(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\mathsf{G}[g_{1},\phi]-\mathsf{G}[g_{2},\phi])(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{3}}(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{T}+\|\mathbf{g}\|_{T})(1+\|(g_{1},g_{2},\phi)\|_{X_{T}})^{2(m+5)}.

Combining this with (
3.17
) yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{X_{T}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}\|_{X_{T}}(\|(g_{1}-\phi,g_{2}-\phi)\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{15}}(1+\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}}))(1+\|(g_{1},g_{2},\phi)\|_{X_{T}}+\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{3(m+5)}

\displaystyle+T^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{T}(1+\|\phi\|_{C^{m+2}}+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{X_{T}}+\|\partial_{x}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{3(m+5)}.

If
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma^{\prime}}
, one has
\|(g_{1}-\phi,g_{2}-\phi)\|_{X_{T}}\leq 2\sigma^{\prime}
and
\|(f_{1}-\phi,f_{2}-\phi)\|_{X_{T}}\leq 2\sigma^{\prime}
. Combining this with (
3.4
) yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{X_{T}}\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{1}(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{X_{T}}+\|\mathbf{g}\|_{X_{T}})(2\sigma^{\prime}+{T}^{\frac{1}{100}}\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}})(1+2\sigma^{\prime}+{T}^{\frac{1}{100}}\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}}+T^{\frac{1}{3}})^{3(m+5)}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Theta(T,\sigma^{\prime},\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}})(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{X_{T}}+\|\mathbf{g}\|_{X_{T}}).

Taking
\varepsilon_{0},\sigma^{\prime},T
defined in (
3.14
) such that
\Theta(T,\sigma^{\prime},\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}})\leq\frac{1}{100}
, then

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{T}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{g}\|_{X_{T}}.

Hence
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}:\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{X}_{T,\phi}^{\sigma^{\prime}}
is a contraction map. This completes the proof of the proposition.

Remark 3.3

The non-endpoint norm
\|f\|_{T,*}
provides the desired smallness to proceed the fixed point argument and obtain the local solution under the condition (
3.4
). More precisely, as we can see in the proof of Proposition
3.1

ii)
, the contraction mapping theorem is performed in the set with center
\phi
. In this case,
\|f\|_{T}
is merely bounded, but the non-endpoint norm

\displaystyle\|f\|_{T,*}\leq\|f-\phi\|_{T,*}+\|\phi\|_{T,*}\lesssim\|f-\phi\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{15}}\|\phi\|_{C^{m+3}},

is small because of the smallness of
\|f-\phi\|_{T,*}
and
T
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

We first prove
i)
under the condition that
\varrho_{0}=0
and (
1.24
) holds.

1: Construction of approximating sequence.

For
\vartheta\in(0,1)
, define
f_{0,\vartheta}=f_{0}\ast\rho_{\vartheta}
, then by Proposition
3.1
, there exists
T_{\vartheta}=T(\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}},\vartheta)
, such that (
1.21
) admits a unique solution denoted by
f_{\vartheta}
in
[0,T_{\vartheta}]
with initial data
f_{0,\vartheta}
. Note that if (
1.24
) holds for
f_{0}
, it also holds for
f_{0,\vartheta}
for any
\vartheta\in(0,1)
.

2: Control of
\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}
.

Applying Theorem
1.1
to (
3.2
) with
(s,b,\eta)=(3,1,\frac{1}{5})
and
\mathsf{A}(t,x,\xi)=\frac{|\xi|^{3}}{\langle\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}(t,x)\rangle^{3}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{L_{T_{\vartheta}}^{\infty}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T_{\vartheta}}(\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}])+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T,*}\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}},

where
\mathsf{Data}_{T_{\vartheta}}
,
\|\cdot\|_{B_{T}}
are defined in (
3.6
) and (
1.11
), respectively.
It follows from Lemma
6.10
that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T_{\vartheta}}(\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}])\lesssim\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}^{2}(1+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta}})^{2m+5}.

(3.18)

Moreover, by definition, it is easy to obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{A}\|_{B_{T}}\lesssim\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}(1+\|f\|_{T_{\vartheta}})^{2m+5}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{L_{T_{\vartheta}}^{\infty}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq C_{0}\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+C_{0}\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}^{2}(1+\|f\|_{T_{\vartheta}})^{2m+5},

where the constant
C_{0}
depends only on
m
.

3: Control of
\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}
.

Applying Theorem
1.1
to (
3.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\|\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{\vartheta}}L^{\infty}})^{m+5}\left(\|f_{0,{\vartheta}}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T_{\vartheta}}(\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}])+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}^{2}\right).

(3.19)

Remark that the additional factor
(1+\|\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{\vartheta}}L^{\infty}})^{m+5}
comes from the underlying lower bound of the coefficient
\frac{1}{\langle\partial_{x}f\rangle^{3}}
in (
3.2
), see Remark
2.7
.
Combining (
3.19
) with (
3.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}\leq C_{0}(1+\|\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{\vartheta}}L^{\infty}})^{m+5}\left(\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}^{2}(1+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta}})^{2m+5}\right),

where the constant
C_{0}
depends only on
m,\kappa,\eta
.

4: Existence.

Take
\varepsilon_{0}:=\frac{1}{(10+C_{0})^{10(m+5)}}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}).
Suppose that the initial data satisfies the quantitative bound

\displaystyle(1+\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{4(m+5)}\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq\varepsilon.

There exist
T_{\vartheta}>0
and a unique solution
f_{\vartheta}
in
[0,T_{\vartheta}]
which satisfies

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta}}\leq 4C_{0}(\varepsilon+\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}),\ \ \ \ \|f_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}\leq 10C_{0}(1+10C_{0}\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{2m+5}\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}.

(3.20)

Furthermore, for the time derivatives, by (
3.2
) and (
3.20
), we obtain

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\vartheta}]}t^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\partial_{t}f_{\vartheta}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}<\infty.

By a bootstrap argument and the existence argument in Proposition
2.9
, we can pass to the limit
\vartheta\rightarrow 0
, and the sequence will converge to
f
, which is a solution to (
3.2
) in
[0,\infty)
. By the a priori estimates above,
f
satisfies

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\infty}\leq C(\varepsilon+\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}),

(3.21)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\infty,*}\leq 10C_{0}(1+10C_{0}\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{2m+5}\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon.

5: Stability and uniqueness.

Suppose
f,g
are solutions to (
3.2
) in
[0,T]
with initial data
f_{0},g_{0}\in\dot{W}^{1,\infty}
which satisfy the quantitative bound (
1.24
).
By making a difference of the equations, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(f-g)+\frac{\Lambda^{3}(f-g)}{\langle\partial_{x}f\rangle^{3}}=\mathsf{N}[f](x)-\mathsf{N}[g](x)-\mathsf{G}[g,f],

(3.22)

\displaystyle(f-g)|_{t=0}=f_{0}-g_{0}.

Applying Theorem
1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|f-g\|_{T}\lesssim(1+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{m+5}(\|f_{0}-g_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{N}[f]-\mathsf{N}[g]-\mathsf{G}[g,f])+\|f-g\|_{T,*}\|f\|_{T,*}).

Lemma
6.10
and Lemma
6.11
yield

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{N}[f]-\mathsf{N}[g])+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{G}[g,f])\lesssim\|f-g\|_{T}\|(f,g)\|_{T,*}(1+\|(f,g)\|_{T})^{2m+5}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f-g\|_{T}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{m+5}(\|f_{0}-g_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\|f-g\|_{T}\|(f,g)\|_{T,*}(1+\|(f,g)\|_{T})^{2m+5}).

The condition (
1.24
) with
\varepsilon\leq(10+C)^{-100}
, together with (
3.21
) yields

C(1+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{m+5}\|(f,g)\|_{T,*}(1+\|(f,g)\|_{T})^{2m+5}\leq\frac{1}{10}.

This implies

\displaystyle\|f-g\|_{T}\lesssim\|f_{0}-g_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}.

(3.23)

Moreover, let
f,g
to be two solutions to (
1.21
) with the same initial data
f_{0}
, then (
3.23
) infers
f=g
, which ensures the uniqueness of the solution.

Now we prove
ii)
. When
\varrho_{0}\neq 0
, the equation (
1.21
) is not homogeneous. We need to control the lower-order norm
\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}
.
So we work on
\|\cdot\|_{X_{T}}
in this setting.

1: Construction of approximating sequence.

To show the existence of solution, we use the standard compactness argument. We will construct the approximating solutions as follows. Denote
f_{0,\vartheta}=f_{0}\ast\rho_{\vartheta}
, then there exist
T_{0,\vartheta}=T_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}},\vartheta)>0
, and a unique solution
f_{\vartheta}
to (
1.21
) in
[0,T_{0,\vartheta}]
with initial data
f_{0,\vartheta}
. Note that (
1.25
) is equivalent to

(1+\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{4(m+5)}\liminf_{\ell\rightarrow 0}\|f_{0}-f_{0,\ell}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon,

for some universal
C
, so we can take a subsequence
\{f_{0,\vartheta}\}
such that
\phi=f_{0,\ell}
for some fixed
\ell
, and for any
\vartheta<\ell
small, it holds

\displaystyle\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}},

\displaystyle(1+\|f_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{4(m+5)}\|f_{0,\vartheta}-f_{0,\ell}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq 2C\varepsilon.

2: Control of
\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}
.

Rewrite (
3.2
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{\vartheta}+\frac{\Lambda^{3}f_{\vartheta}}{\langle\partial_{x}\phi\rangle^{3}}=\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}]+\mathsf{G}[f_{\vartheta},\phi],

\displaystyle f_{\vartheta}|_{t=0}=f_{0,\vartheta},

with
\phi=f_{0,\ell}
fixed in step 1. Applying Theorem
1.1
, we obtain the following a priori estimates.

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{L_{T_{0,\vartheta}}^{\infty}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}(\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}]+\mathsf{G}[f_{\vartheta},\phi]).

Lemma
6.10
and Lemma
6.11
yield

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}(\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}])+\mathsf{Data}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}(\mathsf{G}[f_{\vartheta},\phi])

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}^{2}+T_{0,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}}+\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{T_{0,\vartheta}}\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*})(1+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}}+\|\phi\|_{T_{0,\vartheta}})^{2m+5}.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{L_{T_{0,\vartheta}}^{\infty}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\lesssim\|\partial_{x}f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}

(3.24)

\displaystyle+(\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}^{2}+T_{0,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}}+\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{T_{0,\vartheta}}\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*})(1+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}}+\|\phi\|_{T_{0,\vartheta}})^{2m+5}.

On the other hand, integrating (
3.7
) in time, together with (
6.10
) and Remark
6.12
, we can get

\displaystyle|f_{\vartheta}(t,x)-f_{0,\vartheta}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}(\|\Lambda^{3}f_{\vartheta}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}](\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathsf{G}[f_{\vartheta},\phi](\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}})d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{0}t^{\frac{1}{3}}(1+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}}+\|(f_{\vartheta},\phi)\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}})^{5},\ \ \ \forall t\in[0,T_{0,\vartheta}].

From this we obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\tilde{C}_{0}T_{0,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{3}}(1+\|(f_{\vartheta},\phi)\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}})^{5}.

Combining this with (
3.24
),
we derive

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}W^{1,\infty}}\leq\tilde{C}_{0}\|f_{0,\vartheta}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

(3.25)

\displaystyle+\tilde{C}_{0}\left(\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}(\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}}+\|\phi\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}})+T_{0,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{10}}\right)\left(1+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}}+\|\phi\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}}\right)^{2m+5}.

Here the constant
\tilde{C}_{0}
depends only on
d
,
\kappa
,
m
and
\varrho_{0}
.

3: Control of
\|f-\phi\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}
.

Applying Theorem
1.1
to the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(f_{\vartheta}-\phi)(x)+\frac{\Lambda^{3}(f_{\vartheta}-\phi)(x)}{\langle\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}(x)\rangle^{3}}=\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}](x)-\frac{\Lambda^{3}\phi(x)}{\langle\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}(x)\rangle^{3}},

\displaystyle(f_{\vartheta}-\phi)|_{t=0}=f_{0,\vartheta}-\phi,

we obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}\lesssim(1+\|\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}L^{\infty}})^{m+5}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left(\|f_{0,\vartheta}-\phi\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}(\mathsf{N}[f_{\vartheta}]-\frac{\Lambda^{3}\phi}{\langle\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}\rangle^{3}})+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}\right),

Combining this with Lemma
6.10
yields

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{0}(1+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}})^{m+5}\|f_{0,\vartheta}-\phi\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle+\tilde{C}_{0}(1+\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}})^{3(m+5)}\left(\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{{T_{0,\vartheta}},*}+T_{0,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{20}}(1+\|(f_{\vartheta},\phi)\|_{X_{T_{0,\vartheta}}})\right)^{2}.

(3.26)

4: Existence.

Denote

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}=10\tilde{C}_{0}(1+10\tilde{C}_{0}\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})^{m+5}\|f_{0}-\phi\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}},\quad\quad\quad M_{1}=4\tilde{C}_{0}(\varepsilon_{1}+\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}).

Take
\varepsilon_{0}=(10+\tilde{C}_{0})^{-10(m+5)}
. For any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, if the initial data satisfy the quantitative bound

\displaystyle(1+\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})^{4(m+5)}\|f_{0}-\phi\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq\varepsilon,

then
(1+M_{1})^{3(m+5)}\varepsilon_{1}\leq\frac{M_{1}}{10}
.
Define

\displaystyle T_{1,\vartheta}:=\sup\{T:\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T}}\leq M_{1},\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{T,*}\leq\varepsilon_{1}\}.

We claim that

\displaystyle T_{1,\vartheta}\geq T_{2}:=\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{1+M_{1}+\|\phi\|_{C^{m+5}}}\right)^{1000(m+5)},\quad\forall\vartheta\in(0,1).

(3.27)

Note that since
\phi=f_{0,\ell}
is independent of
\vartheta
, (
3.27
) gives a uniform bound of
T_{1,\vartheta}
for all
\vartheta
. We prove (
3.27
) by contradiction. If the claim is not true, then
T_{1,\vartheta}<T_{2}
for some
\vartheta
. We have

\|\phi\|_{X_{T_{1}}}\leq M_{1},\ \ \|\phi\|_{T_{1},*}\leq\varepsilon_{1}.

It follows from (
3.25
) and (
3.26
) that

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}W^{1,\infty}}\leq\frac{M_{1}}{4}+\tilde{C}_{0}\left(\varepsilon_{1}+T_{1,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{10}}\right)\left(1+2M_{1}\right)^{2(m+5)}\leq\frac{M_{1}}{2},

\displaystyle\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{T_{1,\vartheta},*}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{10}+\tilde{C}_{0}\left(\varepsilon_{1}+T_{1,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{20}}(1+2M_{1})\right)^{2}\left(1+2M_{1}\right)^{3(m+5)}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2}.

This contradicts the definition of
T_{1,\vartheta}
. Thus, we have proved the following uniform local-in-time a priori estimates

\|f_{\vartheta}\|_{X_{T_{2}}}\leq M_{1},\quad\quad\|f_{\vartheta}-\phi\|_{T_{2},*}\leq\varepsilon_{1}.

Recall that
f_{0,\vartheta}=f_{0}\ast\rho_{\vartheta}
,
f_{\vartheta}
is the unique solution to (
1.21
) in
[0,T_{0,\vartheta}]
with initial data
f_{0,\vartheta}
, and

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0,\vartheta}]}\left(\|f_{\vartheta}(t)\|_{W^{1,\infty}}+t^{\frac{m+\kappa}{s}}\|\partial_{x}f_{\vartheta}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}\right)\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}.

Using the equation (
3.2
) and by interpolation inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0,\vartheta}]}t^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\partial_{t}f_{\vartheta}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim C\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}.

Applying the existence argument in Proposition
2.9
, we can see that
\{f_{\vartheta}\}
has a subsequence that converges to a function
f
on
[0,T_{2}]
, which is a solution to (
1.21
) with initial data
f_{0}
, and satisfying the a priori estimate

\|f\|_{T_{2}}\leq 4\tilde{C}_{0}(\varepsilon+\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}),\quad\|f-\phi\|_{T_{2},*}\leq 10\tilde{C}_{0}(1+10\tilde{C}_{0}\|f_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})^{m+5}\|f_{0}-\phi\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}.

5: Stability and uniqueness.

Suppose
f,g
are solutions to (
1.21
) in
[0,T]
with initial data
f_{0},g_{0}\in\dot{W}^{1,\infty}
that satisfy the quantitative bound (
1.25
). Applying Theorem
1.1
to the equation (
3.22
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|f-g\|_{T}\lesssim C(1+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{m+5}\left(\|f_{0}-g_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{N}[f]-\mathsf{N}[g]-\mathsf{G}[g,f])+\|f-g\|_{T,*}\|f\|_{T,*}\right).

Applying Lemma
6.10
and
6.11
again, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{N}[f]-\mathsf{N}[g])

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{G}[g,f])

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f-g\|_{X_{T}}(\|(f,g)\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{10}})(1+\|(f,g)\|_{T})^{2m+5}.

This yields

\displaystyle\|f-g\|_{T}\lesssim

\displaystyle C(1+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{m+5}

(3.28)

\displaystyle\times\left(\|f_{0}-g_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\|f-g\|_{X_{T}}(\|(f,g)\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{10}})(1+\|(f,g)\|_{T})^{2m+5}\right).

To estimate the
L^{\infty}
norm, we integrate (
3.22
) in time, which yields

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x}|(f-g)(t,x)-(f_{0}-g_{0})(x)|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}(\|\Lambda^{3}(f-g)(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\mathsf{N}[f]-\mathsf{N}[g])(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathsf{G}[f,\phi](\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}})d\tau

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{3}}\|f-g\|_{X_{T}}(1+\|(f,g)\|_{X_{T}})^{2m+5}.

This implies

\displaystyle\|f-g\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|f_{0}-g_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+T^{\frac{1}{3}}\|f-g\|_{X_{T}}(1+\|(f,g)\|_{X_{T}})^{2m+5}.

Combining this with (
3.28
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|f-g\|_{X_{T}}\leq C(1+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}})^{m+5}\left(\|f_{0}-g_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}+\|f-g\|_{X_{T}}(\|(f,g)\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{10}})(1+\|(f,g)\|_{T})^{2m+5}\right).

By taking
T,\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, from (
1.25
) we have

\displaystyle C(\|(f,g)\|_{T,*}+T^{\frac{1}{10}})(1+\|(f,g)\|_{T})^{3m+5}\leq\frac{1}{10}.

Thus, we obtain

\|f-g\|_{X_{T}}\leq 2C\|f_{0}-g_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}.

(3.29)

Moreover, if
f,g
are two solutions to (
1.21
) with the same initial data
f_{0}
, then (
3.29
) gives
f=g
, which infers the uniqueness of the solution.

4
The Peskin problem in 2D

In this section, we study the 2D Peskin problem (
4.3
) and prove Theorem
1.9
. We begin by reformulating (
4.3
) in Section
4.1
, distinguishing between the dominant quasilinear part and the lower-order nonlinear terms. Next, in Section
4.2
, we apply Theorem
1.1
and a contraction mapping argument to the reformulated equation to establish local well-posedness. Finally, in Section
4.3
, we provide a brief discussion on the global well-posedness and asymptotic behavior of solutions for initial data close to equilibrium circle configurations.

4.1
Reformulation

For simplicity, denote
\mathbf{T}(\lambda)=\frac{\mathcal{T}(\lambda)}{\lambda}
. The Peskin problem (
1.28
) can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}X(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{S}}G(\delta_{\alpha}X(x))\partial_{x}\left({\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)}\partial_{x}X\right)(x-\alpha)d\alpha

(4.1)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{S}}\partial_{\alpha}G(\delta_{\alpha}X(x))\delta_{\alpha}\left({\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)}\partial_{x}X\right)d\alpha:=\mathsf{N}(X(x)),

where the second line follows from integration by parts. We denote
\tilde{\alpha}=(\frac{1}{2}\cot(\frac{\alpha}{2}))^{-1}
. Note that

\frac{1}{2}\cot(\frac{\alpha}{2})=\frac{1}{\alpha}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha+2n\pi}+\frac{1}{\alpha-2n\pi}\right).

Hence, for any
h(\alpha):\mathbb{S}\to\mathbb{R}
, by using the periodicity
h(\alpha)=h(\alpha+2n\pi)
for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(\alpha)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha}=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}h(\alpha)\frac{d\alpha}{\tilde{\alpha}}.

(4.2)

Following
[
25
]
, we can reformulate the equation as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}X(x)+\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{H}({\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)}\partial_{x}X)(x)=\mathcal{N}(X(x)),

(4.3)

where
\mathcal{H}
is the Hilbert transform on the torus, which is defined by

\mathcal{H}f(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{S}}f(x-\alpha)\cot\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)d\alpha,

and the nonlinear term reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(X)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X\cdot\tilde{\mathsf{E}}^{\alpha}X}{|\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X|^{2}}\delta_{\alpha}({\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)}\partial_{x}X)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha}

(4.4)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tilde{\mathsf{E}}^{\alpha}X\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X+\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X\otimes\tilde{\mathsf{E}}^{\alpha}X}{|\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X|^{2}}\delta_{\alpha}({\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)}\partial_{x}X)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X}{|\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X|^{4}}\left(\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X\cdot\tilde{\mathsf{E}}^{\alpha}X\right)\delta_{\alpha}({\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)}\partial_{x}X)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha}.

Here we denote
