\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X(x)=\frac{\delta_{\alpha}X(x)}{\tilde{\alpha}},\quad\tilde{\mathsf{E}}^{\alpha}X(x)=X^{\prime}(x-\alpha)-\tilde{\Delta}_{\alpha}X(x),\quad X^{\prime}=\partial_{x}X,\quad\tilde{\alpha}=\left(\frac{1}{2}\cot\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)\right)^{-1}.

(4.5)

Taking
\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}
to (
4.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X(x)+\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)}\partial_{x}X)=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(X).

(4.6)

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)}\partial_{x}X)

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|))\partial_{x}X+\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}X-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\delta_{\alpha}(\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|))\delta_{\alpha}\partial_{x}X\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{\frac{3}{2}}}

(4.7)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}X)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}X+\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}X),

where we denote

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}X)=\frac{1}{4}\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)\mathrm{Id}+\frac{\mathbf{T}^{\prime}(|\partial_{x}X|)}{4}\frac{\partial_{x}X\otimes\partial_{x}X}{|\partial_{x}X|},

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}X)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\delta_{\alpha}(\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|))\delta_{\alpha}\partial_{x}X\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{1}{\pi}\partial_{x}X\int_{\mathbb{R}}\delta_{\alpha}(\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|))-\delta_{\alpha}\partial_{x}X\cdot\nabla\big(\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X|)\big)\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

(4.8)

Hence, we rewrite (
4.6
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X+\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}X)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}X=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(X)-\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}X).

(4.9)

Note that

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}(b)=\frac{1}{4}\frac{\mathcal{T}(|b|)}{|b|}\left(\mathrm{Id}-\frac{b\otimes b}{|b|^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(|b|)\frac{b\otimes b}{|b|^{2}}.

From (
1.27
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}(b)\geq\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{c}(|b|)\mathrm{Id},\ \ \ \ |\nabla_{b}^{n}\mathsf{A}(b)|\lesssim_{n}\mathfrak{C}(|b|)(1+|b|^{-n-1}).

(4.10)

4.2
Proof of Theorem
1.9

The proof of Theorem
1.9
is organized in the following stages.

1.

Relaxed version:

We prove Proposition
4.1
as a preliminary case, replacing the Besov norms in (
1.32
) by Lipschitz norms.

2.

Existence theory:

For initial data satisfying (
1.32
), we establish a priori estimates and construct solutions via compactness arguments.

3.

Stability analysis:

We derive continuous dependence on initial data for the obtained solutions.

First, we will prove a relaxed version of Theorem
1.9
.

Proposition 4.1

Fix
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
\kappa\in(0,1)
.
For any initial data
X_{0}\in\dot{W}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S})
with
\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}<\infty
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(\mathbf{\Theta}_{0},\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})>0
such that if

\displaystyle\|X_{0}-\Phi\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq\varepsilon_{0},

(4.11)

holds for some smooth function
\Phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S})
, then there exists
T=T(\varepsilon_{0},\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})>0
such that the 2D Peskin problem (
1.28
) admits a unique solution
X
in

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{T}:

\displaystyle=\big\{Y(t,x):(0,T)\times\mathbb{S}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}:\mathbf{\Theta}_{Y}(T)\leq 2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0},,

\displaystyle\|Y\|_{T}:=\sup_{t\in(0,T)}(\|\partial_{x}Y(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|\partial_{x}Y(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\leq 2\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big\}.

Proof.
We apply Theorem
1.1
along with the nonlinear estimates from Lemmas
6.13
and
6.14
to (
4.9
) to establish well-posedness.

Rewrite (
4.9
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X+\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X)=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(X)+F(\Phi,X),

(4.12)

where

\displaystyle F(\Phi,X)=\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}X)+(\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)-\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}X))\Lambda(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X).

Define

\displaystyle\|h\|_{T,*}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(t^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\partial_{x}h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\frac{1}{10}}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|\partial_{x}h(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}).

(4.13)

Let
\sigma,T>0
. Consider the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}=\left\{Y\in L_{T}^{\infty}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}:Y|_{t=0}=X_{0},\ \|Y-\Phi\|_{T}\leq\sigma\right\}.

(4.14)

By definition and (
4.11
), it is easy to check that
|\partial_{x}\Phi(x)|
has the following upper and lower bound

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}\Phi(x)|\leq\|X_{0}-\Phi\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\|X_{0}\|_{{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}+\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq\frac{3}{2}\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}},

(4.15)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}\Phi(x)|\geq|\partial_{x}X_{0}(x)|-\|X_{0}-\Phi\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\geq\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}^{-1}-\varepsilon_{0}\geq\frac{1}{2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}},\quad\quad\forall\ x\in\mathbb{S},

provided
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{100\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}},\frac{1}{2}\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\}
.
Taking

\sigma\leq\min\{\frac{1}{100\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}},\frac{1}{2}\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\},

it is straightforward to check that
for any
Y\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}_{T,\Phi}
, it holds
\|Y-\Phi\|_{T}\leq\sigma
and

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha,x}|\Delta_{\alpha}Y(x)|

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\alpha,x}|\Delta_{\alpha}X_{0}(x)|-\|\partial_{x}(X_{0}-\Phi)\|_{L^{\infty}}-\|\partial_{x}(\Phi-Y)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}}-\frac{5}{4}\varepsilon_{0}-\sigma\geq\frac{1}{2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}},

(4.16)

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Theta}_{Y}(T)\leq 2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0},\ \ \ \|\partial_{x}Y\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}\leq 2\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},\ \ \forall Y\in\mathcal{X}^{\sigma}_{T,\Phi}.

(4.17)

Combining this with (
4.10
), we obtain

\displaystyle\inf_{\tau\in[0,1]}\mathsf{A}(\tau\partial_{x}Y+(1-\tau)\partial_{x}\Phi)\geq\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{c}(2\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})\mathrm{Id},

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}|(\nabla^{n}\mathsf{A})(\tau\partial_{x}Y+(1-\tau)\partial_{x}\Phi)|\lesssim\mathfrak{C}(2\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})(1+10\mathbf{\Theta}_{0})^{n+1},\ \ \forall n\in\mathbb{N},

where
\mathfrak{c}(\lambda),\mathfrak{C}(\lambda)
are defined in (
1.27
). Denote

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{T}(Y):=\left(1+(\mathfrak{c}(\|\partial_{x}Y\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}))^{-1}+\mathfrak{C}(\|\partial_{x}Y\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}})(1+\mathbf{\Theta}_{Y}(T))^{m+1}\right)^{m+2},

(4.18)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0}:=\left(1+(\mathfrak{c}(1+4\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}))^{-1}+\mathfrak{C}(1+4\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})(1+10\mathbf{\Theta}_{0})^{m+1}\right)^{m+2}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{T}(Y)\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0},\ \ \ \quad\quad\forall Y\in\mathcal{X}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}.

(4.19)

For any
Y\in\mathcal{X}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}
, where
T,\sigma
will be fixed later, we define a map
\mathcal{S}Y=X
, where
X
solves the Cauchy problem

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X+\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X)=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(Y)+F(\Phi,Y),

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X|_{t=0}=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}X_{0}.

Step 1:
We first prove that there exist
\sigma,T>0
such that
\mathcal{S}
maps
\mathcal{X}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}
to itself.
Denote
\tilde{X}=X-\Phi
, one has

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\tilde{X}+\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\tilde{X})=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(Y)+F(\Phi,Y)-\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}\Phi.

(4.20)

Applying Corollary
2.6
to (
4.20
) with
b=\frac{1}{2}
, we obtain that there exists
T_{0}=T_{0}(\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})>0
such that for any
0<T\leq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{X}\|_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}\left(\|\tilde{X}_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(Y)+F(\Phi,Y)+\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}\Phi)\right),

(4.21)

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(f)=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(t^{\kappa}\|f(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|f(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa-\frac{1}{2}}}).

By Lemma
6.13
and (
4.19
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(Y))\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0}(\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+2}})^{2}(1+\|Y\|_{T})^{2(m+5)},

(4.22)

where
\tilde{Y}=Y-\Phi
. Here we also used the fact that
\|Y\|_{T,*}\leq\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T,*}+\|\Phi\|_{T,*}\lesssim\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+2}}
.

Then we estimate
F(\Phi,Y)
. We first deal with
\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}Y)
.
By Lemma
6.14
and (
4.19
), we have for any
0<T<1
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}Y))\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}(\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+2}})(1+\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+\|\Phi\|_{T})^{m+5}.

From Lemma
6.5
, it is straightforward to obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}((\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)-\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}Y))\Lambda(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}Y))\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}(\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2}(1+\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+\|\Phi\|_{T})^{m+5}.

Hence, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(F(\Phi,Y))\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}(\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2}(1+\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+\|\Phi\|_{T})^{m+5}.

It remains to estimate the lower order term
\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}\Phi
.
It is easy to check that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}\Phi)\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}T^{\kappa}(1+\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{m+3}.

(4.23)

Combining (
4.22
)-(
4.23
) with (
4.21
), we obtain for any
0<T\leq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{X}\|_{T}\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|\tilde{X}_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}(\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+T^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2}(1+\|\tilde{Y}\|_{T}+\|\partial_{x}\Phi\|_{T})^{2(m+5)}

(4.24)

\displaystyle+C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}T^{\kappa}(1+\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2(m+5)}.

Note that by (
4.15
),

\displaystyle\|\Phi\|_{T}\leq\|\partial_{x}\Phi\|_{L^{\infty}}+T^{m+\kappa}\|\partial_{x}\Phi\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}\leq 1+\frac{3}{2}\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+T^{m+\kappa}\|\Phi\|_{\dot{C}^{m+2}}.

(4.25)

Take
0<\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{1}{100}(C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}+\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+1)^{-m-5}
,
\sigma=2C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}
, and

T_{1}=\min\left\{T_{0},\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{10+C_{0}+\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}}}\right)^{100m}\right\}.

Then (
4.24
) leads to

\displaystyle\|\tilde{X}\|_{T_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}+2C_{0}^{2}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}\varepsilon_{0}(2C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}\varepsilon_{0}+T_{1}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})(1+\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+T^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2(m+5)}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}T_{1}^{\kappa}(1+\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2(m+5)}\leq\sigma.

Hence
\mathcal{S}
maps
\mathcal{X}_{T_{1},\Phi}^{\sigma}
to itself.

Step 2:
We prove that
\mathcal{S}
is a contraction map. Consider
Y_{1},Y_{2}\in\mathcal{X}_{T_{1},\Phi}^{\sigma}
. Denote
\mathbf{Y}=Y_{1}-Y_{2}
, and
\mathbf{X}=X_{1}-X_{2}=\mathcal{S}Y_{1}-\mathcal{S}Y_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathbf{X}+\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathbf{X})=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{N}(Y_{1})-\mathcal{N}(Y_{2}))+F(\Phi,Y_{1})-F(\Phi,Y_{2}).

Applying Theorem
1.1
, we have for any
0<T\leq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{X}\|_{T}\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0}\left(\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{N}(Y_{1})-\mathcal{N}(Y_{2})))+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(F(\Phi,Y_{1})-F(\Phi,Y_{2}))\right).

From Lemma
6.13
and
6.14
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{N}(Y_{1})-\mathcal{N}(Y_{2})))

\displaystyle+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(F(\Phi,Y_{1})-F(\Phi,Y_{2}))

(4.26)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{T}\|({Y}_{1},{Y}_{2})\|_{T,*}(1+\|(Y_{1},Y_{2})\|_{T})^{2(m+1)}.

Hence, by (
4.25
),

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{X}\|_{T_{1}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{T_{1}}(\|(\tilde{Y}_{1},\tilde{Y}_{2})\|_{T_{1}}+T_{1}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})

\displaystyle\times(1+\|(\tilde{Y}_{1},\tilde{Y}_{2})\|_{T_{1}}+\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2(m+5)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4C_{1}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}\sigma(1+4\sigma+T_{1}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2(m+5)}\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{T_{1}},

where
\tilde{Y}_{i}=Y_{i}-\Phi
, and we used the fact that
\|\tilde{Y}_{1}\|_{T_{1}},\|\tilde{Y}_{2}\|_{T_{1}}\leq\sigma
and
\mathbf{\Theta}_{Y_{1}}(T_{1}),\mathbf{\Theta}_{Y_{2}}(T_{1})\leq 2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}
. We have

\displaystyle 4C_{1}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}\sigma(1+4\sigma+T_{1}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2(m+5)}\leq\frac{1}{2}

by taking
\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{1}{100}(C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}+C_{1}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}+\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+1)^{-2(m+10)}
and the corresponding
T
small enough. This implies
\mathcal{S}:\mathcal{X}_{T_{1},\Phi}^{\sigma}\to\mathcal{X}_{T_{1},\Phi}^{\sigma}
is a contraction map. Hence, there exists a unique
X\in\mathcal{X}_{T_{1},\Phi}^{\sigma}
such that
X=\mathcal{S}X
, which indicates
X
is a solution to (
4.3
). Moreover, by (
4.17
) we have

\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T_{1})\leq 2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}
. This completes the proof of the Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.9
.

Step 1: Construction of approximating sequence.

To show the existence of solution, we use the standard compactness argument. We will construct the approximating solutions as follows. Denote
X_{0,\vartheta}=X_{0}\ast\rho_{\vartheta}
, and denote
X_{\vartheta}
to be the solution of (
1.28
) with initial data
X_{0,\vartheta}
on
[0,T_{\vartheta}]
with
T_{\vartheta}=T(\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}},\vartheta)
. Note that (
1.32
) implies

\mathfrak{M}(z_{0})\liminf_{\ell\rightarrow 0}\|X_{0}-X_{0,\ell}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon.

We fix an
\ell\ll 1
and let
\Phi=X_{0,\ell}
. There exists a subsequence
\{X_{0,\vartheta}\}
,
\vartheta<\ell
, such that

\displaystyle\|X_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq 2\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(z_{0})\|X_{0,\vartheta}-X_{0,\ell}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq 2\varepsilon.

Furthermore, we can take
\vartheta
small enough such that for any
\alpha,x\in\mathbb{S}
,
\alpha\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{|\delta_{\alpha}X_{0,\vartheta}(x)|}{|\alpha|}\geq\frac{1}{2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}}.

This implies that

\mathbf{\Theta}(X_{0,\vartheta})\leq 2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}.

(4.27)

Step 2: Control of
\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{\vartheta}}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}
.

Applying Corollary
2.6
to (
4.9
) with
b=\frac{1}{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\lesssim\|X_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T_{\vartheta}}(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(X_{\vartheta})-\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta}))+\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T,*}^{2},

(4.28)

where we denote
\Phi=X_{0,\ell}
as fixed in step 1. It follows from Lemma
6.13
and Lemma
6.14
that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}_{T_{\vartheta}}(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(X_{\vartheta})-\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta}))\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}^{2}(1+\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta}})^{2(m+5)}.

(4.29)

The estimates (
4.28
) and (
4.29
) give that

\displaystyle\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{\vartheta}}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left(\|X_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathfrak{M}_{0}(\|\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}+T_{\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+3}})^{2}(1+\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta}})^{2(m+5)}\right).

(4.30)

Step 3: Control of
\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}
.

From (
4.9
), we have for
\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}=X_{\vartheta}-\Phi
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}(x)+\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta})\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(X_{\vartheta})-\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta})-\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta})\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}\Phi.

It follows from Corollary
2.6
and (
4.29
) that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}\left(\|\tilde{X}_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{1}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T_{\vartheta}}(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{N}(X_{\vartheta})-\mathsf{M}(\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta}))+\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}\|\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}\right)

(4.31)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\mathfrak{M}_{0}(\|\tilde{X}_{0,\vartheta}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{1}}+\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|(\tilde{X}_{\vartheta},\Phi)\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}^{2}(1+\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta}})^{2(m+5)}+\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}\|\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}).

Step 4: Control of
\mathbf{\Theta}_{X_{\vartheta}}(T_{\vartheta})
.

Now we control
\mathbf{\Theta}_{X_{\vartheta}}(T_{\vartheta})
for
\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}
data satisfying (
1.32
), which differs from the case of Lipschitz data addressed in estimate (
4.2
), due to the absence of smallness in the Lipschitz norm. We therefore employ the approach outlined in
[
25
]
. Define

\displaystyle Q_{h}(T)=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{\alpha,s}\frac{|\alpha|^{\tilde{\varepsilon}}}{t^{\tilde{\varepsilon}}}\left|\frac{1}{|\Delta_{\alpha}h(t,x)|}-\frac{1}{|\Delta_{\alpha}h(0,x)|}\right|,

where
0<\tilde{\varepsilon}<\frac{1}{10}
is a fixed parameter.
By (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{|\Delta_{\alpha}X_{\vartheta}(t,\cdot)(x)|}-\frac{1}{|\Delta_{\alpha}X_{0,\vartheta}(x)|}\right|=\int_{0}^{t}\left|\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{|\Delta_{\alpha}X_{\vartheta}(\tau,\cdot)(x)|}\right)\right|d\tau

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\mathbf{\Theta}^{2}_{X_{\vartheta}}(t)\int_{0}^{t}|\Delta_{\alpha}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta}|)\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta})(\tau,\cdot)(x)|+|\Delta_{\alpha}\mathcal{N}(X_{\vartheta}(\tau,\cdot))(x)|d\tau.

The integral on the right hand side can be controlled by
\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle Q_{X_{\vartheta}}(T_{\vartheta})\leq\mathbf{\Theta}_{X_{\vartheta}}^{2}(T_{\vartheta})\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0,\vartheta}]}t^{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}}(\|\mathbf{T}(|\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta}|)\partial_{x}X_{\vartheta}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}}}+\|\mathcal{N}(X_{\vartheta}(t))\|_{\dot{C}^{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}}})

(4.32)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\mathbf{\Theta}_{X_{\vartheta}}(T_{\vartheta}))^{10}(1+\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta}})^{10}\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{\vartheta},*},

By
[
25
, Lemma 2.8]
, we know that the smallness of
Q_{X_{\vartheta}}(T_{\vartheta})
implies the boundedness of
\mathbf{\Theta}_{X_{\vartheta}}(T_{0,\vartheta})
.

Step 5: Existence.

We define the increasing function
\mathfrak{M}:[0,\infty)\to[1,\infty)
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(\lambda)=\tilde{C}\left((\mathfrak{c}(\tilde{C}\lambda))^{-1}+\mathfrak{C}(\tilde{C}\lambda)+\lambda+1\right)^{10(m+1)^{3}},

(4.33)

where
\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{C}
are defined in (
1.27
), and
\tilde{C}
is a fixed constant which is large enough to dominate all absolute constants appeared in this proof. Take
\varepsilon_{0}=(10+C_{0}+C_{1})^{-10(m+5)}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
.
Denote

M_{1}=4C_{0}(\varepsilon+\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}),\quad\varepsilon_{1}=4C_{1}\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|\tilde{X}_{0}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,\infty}^{1}}.

With the definition of
\mathfrak{M}(\lambda)
in (
4.33
), the condition (
1.32
) implies

\displaystyle(1+M_{1})\varepsilon_{1}\leq\varepsilon.

(4.34)

Define

T_{0,\vartheta}=\sup\{T:\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq M_{1},\ \|\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}\|_{T,*}\leq\varepsilon_{1},\ \mathbf{\Theta}_{X_{\vartheta}}(T)\leq 10\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}\}.

We claim that

T_{0,\vartheta}\geq T_{1}:=\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{10+\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+5}}}\right)^{1000(m+5)}.

(4.35)

Note that since we fix
\Phi=X_{0,\ell}
for some
\ell
, (
4.35
) gives a uniform lower bound of
T_{0,\vartheta}
for any
\vartheta
. In fact, by contradiction, if
T_{0,\vartheta}<T_{1}
, then by (
4.30
), (
4.31
) and (
4.34
), we have

\displaystyle\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{0,\vartheta}}\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq\frac{M_{1}}{4}+\mathfrak{M}_{0}(\varepsilon_{1}^{2}+T_{0,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{\dot{C}^{m+5}})\leq\frac{M_{1}}{2},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{0,\vartheta},*}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{4}+\mathfrak{M}_{0}(\varepsilon_{1}^{2}+T_{0,\vartheta}^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\Phi\|_{\dot{C}^{m+5}})\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2}.

By (
4.27
), (
4.32
) and
[
25
, Lemma 2.8]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Theta}_{X_{\vartheta}}(T_{0,\vartheta})\leq 2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0,\vartheta}\leq 4\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}.

which contradicts the choice of
T_{0,\vartheta}
. Thus, the sequence
\{X_{\vartheta}\}_{\vartheta}
have the following uniform a priori bounds:

\|X_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{1}}\leq M_{1},\quad\|\tilde{X}_{\vartheta}\|_{T_{1},*}\leq\varepsilon_{1},\quad\mathbf{\Theta}_{X_{\vartheta}}(T_{1})\leq 10\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}.

Then following the existence argument in Proposition
2.9
, we can pass to the limit
\vartheta\to 0
and the sequence
\{X_{\vartheta}\}_{\vartheta}
will converge to a solution
X
on
[0,T_{1}]
, which is a solution to (
4.3
) with initial data
X_{0}
, and

\displaystyle\|X\|_{T_{1}}\leq M_{1},\quad\|\tilde{X}\|_{T_{1},*}\leq\varepsilon_{1},\quad\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T_{1})\leq 10\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}.

(4.36)

Step 6: Stability and uniqueness.

establish a stability result in the Lipschitz space. Assume
X,Y
are two solutions of (
4.12
) in
[0,T]
with initial data
X_{0},Y_{0}
respectively, both satisfying (
1.32
). Subtracting the corresponding equations, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(X-Y)+\mathsf{A}(\partial_{x}\Phi)\Lambda\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(X-Y)

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{N}(X)-\mathcal{N}(Y))+F(\Phi,X)-F(\Phi,Y),

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(X-Y)|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(X_{0}-Y_{0}).

By (
2.35
), (
4.26
) and (
4.36
), we have

\displaystyle\|X-Y\|_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|X_{0}-Y_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}_{T}(\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{N}(X)-\mathcal{N}(Y))+F(\Phi,X)-F(\Phi,Y))

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|X_{0}-Y_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}\|X-Y\|_{T}\|(X,Y)\|_{T,*}(1+\|(X,T)\|_{T})^{2(m+1)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\mathfrak{M}_{0}\|X_{0}-Y_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+C_{3}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}\varepsilon_{1}(1+M_{1})^{2(m+1)}\|X-Y\|_{T}.

The condition (
1.32
) with
\varepsilon<(1+C_{3})^{-10}
implies
C_{3}\mathfrak{M}_{0}^{2}\varepsilon_{1}(1+M_{1})^{2(m+1)}\leq\frac{1}{10}
.
This yields

\|X-Y\|_{T}\lesssim\|X_{0}-Y_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}.

(4.37)

Moreover, if
X,Y
are two solutions of (
4.12
) with the same initial data
X_{0}
, then (
4.37
) gives that
X=Y
, which ensures the uniqueness of the solution.

4.3
Global existence and asymptotic behavior

Theorem
1.9
establishes the local well-posedness for the 2D Peskin problem (
4.3
). We now outline how this result can be extended to a global existence framework.

It is known that the only stationary solutions to the 2D Peskin system (
4.1
) are uniformly parameterized circles forming a four-dimensional vector space (see
[
72
]
). When interpreting
X(x)
as a complex-valued function
X=X_{1}+iX_{2}
, the stationary solution set can be expressed as

\{X(x)=a_{1}+a_{2}e^{ix}:a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{C},a_{2}\neq 0\}:=\mathcal{V}\backslash\{0\}.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be the
L^{2}
projection onto the space
\mathcal{V}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{Z}(t,x)=z_{0}(t)+z_{1}(t)e^{ix},\ \ \ \text{where}\ z_{0}(t)=\langle Z(t),1\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S})},z_{1}(t)=\left\langle Z(t),e^{ix}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S})}.

Recently, a remarkable work by García-Juárez and Haziot
[
60
]
(see also
[
89
]
) demonstrates that the linearized operator around the unit circle
Z=e^{ix}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}X=\left.\frac{d\mathsf{N}(Z+\varepsilon X)}{d\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

satisfies the dissipative estimate

\langle\mathcal{L}Y,Y\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S})}\leq-2c_{0}\|Y\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S})}^{2},\quad\quad~~\forall\ Y\in\mathcal{V}^{\perp}.

As a consequence, the Green’s function
K(t,x,y)
associated with
(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L})
satisfies the pointwise estimate:

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{l_{1}}\partial_{y}^{l_{2}}(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})K(t,x,y)|\lesssim_{l_{1},l_{2}}\frac{e^{-\frac{3}{2}c_{0}t}}{t^{1+l_{1}+l_{2}}}\left\langle\frac{|x-y|}{t}\right\rangle^{-(l_{1}+l_{2}+3)},\ \ \quad\quad\forall\ l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{N}.

(4.38)

We denote by
e^{t\mathcal{L}}
the semigroup generated by
\mathcal{L}
and apply the freezing coefficient method to obtain short-time Schauder estimates:

\displaystyle\|e^{t\mathcal{L}}\partial_{x}X(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{m}}\lesssim t^{-(m+1-\kappa)}\|X(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}},\ \ \ \quad 0<t<T_{0},

(4.39)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{\tau\mathcal{L}}\partial_{x}X(\tau)d\tau\right\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}\lesssim\sup_{\tau\in(0,t)}\|X(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}},\ \ \ \quad 0<t<T_{0},

(4.40)

for some
\kappa\in(0,1)
, with
T_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
depending on
\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}
and
\|X_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}
.
Combining (
4.39
) with (
4.38
), we have

\displaystyle\|e^{t\mathcal{L}}\partial_{x}X(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{m}}\lesssim e^{-c_{0}t}t^{-(m+1-\kappa)}\|X(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}},\ \ \ \forall t>0,\ \ X\in\mathcal{V}^{\perp}.

(4.41)

Adjusted to the above estimates, we have the following Schauder-type estimate, which is an analog of Proposition
2.9
.

Lemma 4.2

Consider the linear equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Y-\mathcal{L}Y=\partial_{x}F,\quad\quad\text{in}\ (0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{S},

\displaystyle Y|_{t=0}:=Y_{0},

with
Y_{0},F\in\mathcal{V}^{\perp}
.
Let
\kappa\in(0,1)
. For any
T>0
, the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{c_{0}t}(\|Y(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|Y(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\lesssim\|Y_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{c_{0}t}(t^{\kappa}\|F(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|F(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}).

Proof.
The proof basically follows the proof of Proposition
2.9
, the only difference is the appearance of the exponential temporal weight for large time. It suffices to consider
t>1
. Firstly, we have the Duhamel principle:

\displaystyle Y(t)

\displaystyle=e^{t\mathcal{L}}Y_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-\tau)\mathcal{L}}\partial_{x}F(\tau)d\tau:=Y_{L}(t)+Y_{F}(t).

Note that
Y=(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})Y
provided
Y_{0},F\in\mathcal{V}^{\perp}
.
From the estimate of (
4.38
), it is straightforward to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{c_{0}t}(\|Y_{L}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|Y_{L}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})\lesssim\|Y_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

For the contribution of force term, we have

\displaystyle\|Y_{F}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{esexp}}{\lesssim}e^{-c_{0}t}\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-(1-\kappa)}\tau^{-\kappa}d\tau\sup_{\sigma\in[0,T]}e^{c_{0}\sigma}\sigma^{\kappa}\|F(\sigma)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-c_{0}t}\sup_{\sigma\in[0,T]}e^{c_{0}\sigma}\sigma^{\kappa}\|F(\sigma)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}}.

Then we consider the higher order Hölder norm. By (
4.40
) and (
4.41
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|Y_{F}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T_{0}}\|e^{\tau\mathcal{L}}\partial_{x}F(t-\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}d\tau+\int_{T_{0}}^{t}\|e^{\tau\mathcal{L}}\partial_{x}F(t-\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\tau\in(0,T_{0})}\|F(t-\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}}+\int_{T_{0}}^{t}e^{-c_{0}\tau}\tau^{-(m+1)}\|F(t-\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-c_{0}t}t^{-(m+\kappa)}\sup_{\sigma\in(0,t)}e^{c_{0}\sigma}\sigma^{m+\kappa}\|F(\sigma)\|_{C^{m+\kappa}},

where the implicit constant depends on
T_{0}
.
This completes the proof of the lemma.

We now consider the reformulated system for
(Y,Z)=((\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X,\mathcal{P}X)
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Y-\mathcal{L}Y

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})\mathfrak{N}(X),

(4.42)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}\mathfrak{N}(X).

where
\mathfrak{N}(X)=\mathsf{N}(X)-\mathcal{L}X
.

For the nonlinear term
\mathfrak{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{c_{0}t}

\displaystyle(t^{\kappa}\|\mathfrak{N}(X)(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|\mathfrak{N}(X)(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})

(4.43)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}^{2}(1+\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}+\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T)+\|\partial_{x}Z\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}})^{m+3},

with the time-weighted norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}
defined as

\displaystyle\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{\frac{c_{0}t}{4}}(\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}X\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X\|_{\dot{C}^{m+1+\kappa}}).

The proof follows similarly to Lemma
6.13
and Lemma
6.14
, we omit details here and refer to
[
25
]
for similar estimates. Remark that the solution (as well as the nonlinear term) generates the exponential decay property from the Green function (
4.38
).

Consider the Cauchy problem (
4.1
) with initial data
X_{0}
satisfying

(1+\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{10m}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}.

(4.44)

Theorem
1.9
guarantees the local existence of solution
X
in
[0,T^{\prime}_{0}]
with the estimate

\displaystyle\|X\|_{T^{\prime}_{0}}\lesssim(1+\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{10m},

\displaystyle\|X-\mathcal{P}X_{0}\|_{T^{\prime}_{0},*}\leq C(1+\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{10m}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}.

Furthermore, from (
4.42
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}X(T^{\prime}_{0})-\mathcal{P}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}_{0}}\|\mathcal{P}\mathfrak{N}(X)(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau\lesssim T^{\prime}_{0}(1+\|X\|_{T_{0}^{\prime}})^{10m}.

It then follows that

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X\|_{T^{\prime}_{0},*}\lesssim C(1+\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{10m}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}},

which implies

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X(T^{\prime}_{0})\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X(T^{\prime}_{0})\|_{\dot{C}^{1+\eta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(T^{\prime}_{0})^{-\eta}(1+\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{10m}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}.

Note that
T^{\prime}_{0}
is independent of
\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}
. By (
4.44
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X(T^{\prime}_{0})\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}\leq C(T_{0}^{\prime})^{-\eta}\varepsilon_{0}.

(4.45)

Thus, to obtain global well-posedness of solution with initial data satisfying (
4.44
), it suffices to extend the solution globally starting at time
T^{\prime}_{0}
, where the data already has a small Lipschitz norm.

To obtain the global existence and asymptotic behavior of the solution, we separately estimate
Y
and
Z
in the system
(
4.42
) with initial data
(Y_{0},Z_{0})=((\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X(T^{\prime}_{0}),\mathcal{P}X(T_{0}))
. Denote
\tilde{t}=t-T^{\prime}_{0}
. Applying Lemma
4.2
for
\partial_{x}Y
, by (
4.43
) we derive for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{t}\in[0,T]}e^{c_{0}\tilde{t}}

\displaystyle(\|\partial_{x}Y(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\tilde{t}^{m+\kappa}\|\partial_{x}^{m+1}Y(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{x}Y_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{\tilde{t}\in[0,T]}e^{c_{0}\tilde{t}}(\tilde{t}^{\kappa}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})\mathfrak{N}(X)(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa}}+\tilde{t}^{m+\kappa}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})\mathfrak{N}(X)(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\kappa}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{x}Y_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}^{2}(1+\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}+\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T)+\|\partial_{x}Z\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}})^{m+3}.

For
Z
, we directly obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}Z(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}=\|\mathcal{P}\mathfrak{N}(X)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim e^{-c_{0}\tilde{t}}\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}^{2}(1+\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}+\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T)+\|\partial_{x}Z\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}})^{m+3}.

Hence, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}\leq C\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X(T^{\prime}_{0})\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+C\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}^{2}(1+\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}+\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T)+\|\partial_{x}Z\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}})^{m+3}.

Denote

\displaystyle T_{1}=

\displaystyle\sup\left\{T:\|X\|_{\mathcal{G}_{T}}\leq 10C\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X(T^{\prime}_{0})\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}},\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T)\leq 10C\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T^{\prime}_{0}),\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.\|\partial_{x}Z\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}\leq 10C\|\partial_{x}Z(T^{\prime}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right\}.

By standard bootstrap argument, there exists
\varepsilon_{1}>0
such that if
\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X(T^{\prime}_{0})\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}<\varepsilon_{1}
, then
T_{1}=\infty
.
Combining this with (
4.45
), we obtain the global existence of solution with initial data satisfying (
4.44
) with
\varepsilon_{0}\leq(1+C)^{-1}(T^{\prime}_{0})^{\eta}\varepsilon_{1}
. Moreover, the global solution converges
exponentially in time to a stationary circle solution. Specifically, the deviation
Y(t,x)=(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X(t,x)

has the following decay estimate:

\displaystyle\|Y(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|Y(t)\|_{\dot{W}^{1,\infty}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|Y(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{m+1+\kappa}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq C^{\prime}(1+\|\partial_{x}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{10m}\|(\mathrm{Id}-\mathcal{P})X_{0}\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}e^{-\frac{c_{0}t}{8}},

for all
t>0.

5
The Peskin problem in 3D

Due to the non-trivial geometry in the 3D setting, we need to work in local charts. This makes the problem more complicated in view of the non-local character of the equation. However, we mention that the essential structure of the 3D problem is the same as that of the 2D problem.

5.1
Reformulation

In this section, we briefly denote

\widetilde{\nabla}=\nabla_{\mathbb{S}^{2}},\quad\mathbf{T}(\lambda)=\frac{\mathcal{T}\left(\lambda\right)}{\lambda}.

By the standard stereographic projection
\mathcal{X}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}^{2}
, see
[
61
, Definition 3.9]
, we can
transform the equation from
\mathbb{S}^{2}
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Define

\mathcal{X}(\theta)=\left(\frac{2\theta_{1}}{1+|\theta|^{2}},\frac{2\theta_{2}}{1+|\theta|^{2}},\frac{-1+|\theta|^{2}}{1+|\theta|^{2}}\right).

We can see that for any compact set
K\Subset\mathbb{S}^{2}
with
(0,0,1)\notin K
,
\mathcal{X}
is a homeomorphism from
\mathcal{X}^{-1}(K)
to
K
. By local chart, for
F\in C^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
with
(0,0,1)\notin\text{Supp}(F)
and
\widehat{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{x}}}=\mathcal{X}(\theta)
, we can define
\tilde{\nabla}F
as

\widetilde{\nabla}F(\widehat{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{x}}})=\left(\frac{1+|\theta|^{2}}{2}\right)^{2}\sum_{i}\frac{\partial(F\circ\mathcal{X})}{\partial\theta_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}}{\partial\theta_{i}}(\theta),

which naturally gives

|\widetilde{\nabla}F|(\widehat{\mathbf{\boldsymbol{x}}})=\frac{1+|\theta|^{2}}{2}\left|\nabla(F\circ\mathcal{X})\right|(\theta).

Throughout this section, we denote variables on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
by
\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}
, and their counterparts on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
by
\theta,\eta
. With a slight abuse of notation,
L^{\infty}
will refer to either
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
or
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, depending on the domain of the variables under consideration. The same convention applies to
C^{\kappa}
spaces.

Denote
\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*}=(0,0,-1)
. For any
\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we define the rotation
\mathcal{R}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}:\mathbb{S}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}^{2}
such that
\mathcal{R}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*})=\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}
, and define the smooth cut-off functions
0\leq\chi_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}},\tilde{\chi}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}\leq 1
such that
\mathrm{Supp}(\chi_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}})\subset\tilde{\chi}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}^{-1}(1)
, and

\displaystyle\text{Supp}(\chi_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}\circ\mathcal{R}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}})\subset B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*},a_{2})\cap\mathbb{S}^{2},\quad\chi_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}\circ\mathcal{R}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}(B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*},a_{1})\cap\mathbb{S}^{2})=1,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\text{Supp}(\tilde{\chi}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}\circ\mathcal{R}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}})\subset B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*},a_{4})\cap\mathbb{S}^{2},\quad\tilde{\chi}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}}\circ\mathcal{R}_{x_{0}}(B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*},a_{3})\cap\mathbb{S}^{2})=1,

\displaystyle B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*},a_{1})\cap\mathbb{S}^{2}\subset\mathcal{X}(B(0,R_{0}))\subset\mathcal{X}(B(0,2R_{0}))\subset B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*},a_{2})\cap\mathbb{S}^{2},

\displaystyle B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*},a_{3})\cap\mathbb{S}^{2}\subset\mathcal{X}(B(0,3R_{0}))\subset\mathcal{X}(B(0,4R_{0}))\subset B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{*},a_{4})\cap\mathbb{S}^{2},

where
0<a_{1}<a_{2}<a_{3}<a_{4}
,
B(a,r)
denotes the ball with center
a
and radius
r
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
or
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and
R_{0}
is a fixed constant in this section. We take finite
\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\subset\mathbb{S}^{2}
and
\{\chi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}=\{\chi_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{n}
such that
\cup_{i=1}^{n}\chi_{i}^{-1}(1)
is a cover of
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, and denote
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\mathcal{R}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}}
.

Define the Hölder norm on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
as

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|(f\chi_{i})\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

(5.2)

Note that the definition is equivalent to the normal definition of inhomogeneous Hölder norm on sphere.

Let
\Phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
close to
F_{0}
in the sense of
C^{1}
that will be fixed later, we rewrite the equation of
(F-\Phi)
as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(F-\Phi)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})+\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}(F-\Phi)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})=N(F,\Phi)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}),

(5.3)

\displaystyle(F-\Phi)|_{t=0}=X_{0}-\Phi

We give the formula of the pseudo-differential operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}
and the nonlinear terms
N(F,\Phi)
as the following.

The Pseudo-differential operator.
The operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}
is defined by

\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}H(\hat{x})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}\cdot(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}H)(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}),

(5.4)

where
G
is defined in (
1.30
), and
J=J_{1}+J_{2}
is a
3^{\otimes 4}
tensor, which is defined by Einstein notation as

\displaystyle(J_{1}(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi))^{ij}_{kl}=\mathbf{T}(|\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi|)\delta_{ki}\delta_{lj},

(5.5)

\displaystyle(J_{2}(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi))^{ij}_{kl}=\mathbf{T}^{\prime}(|\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi|)\frac{(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi^{k})_{l}(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi^{i})_{j}}{|\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi|},

where
\delta_{ij}=1
if
i=j
and
\delta_{ij}=0
otherwise. Note that
(J_{\sigma}(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}(F-\Phi))_{ij}=\sum_{k,l}J_{\sigma}(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)^{ij}_{kl}(\widetilde{\nabla}(F-\Phi)^{k})_{l}
for
\sigma=1,2
.

Nonlinear terms.
The nonlinear term
N(F,\Phi)
can be decomposed as

N(F,\Phi)=(N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3})(F,\Phi)

with the following definitions

\displaystyle N_{1}(F,\Phi)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})=-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\big(G(F(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-F(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))-G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\big)\widetilde{\nabla}\cdot(\mathbf{T}(|\widetilde{\nabla}F|)\widetilde{\nabla}F)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}),

(5.6)

\displaystyle N_{2}(F,\Phi)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})=-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\widetilde{\nabla}\cdot\big(\mathbf{T}(|\widetilde{\nabla}F|)\widetilde{\nabla}F-\mathbf{T}(|\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi|)\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi-J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}(F-\Phi)\big)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}),

\displaystyle N_{3}(\Phi)(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})=-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\widetilde{\nabla}\cdot(\mathbf{T}(|\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi|)\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}).

Then (
5.3
) follows from definition above.
Now we verify conditions (
2.54
) and (
2.55
) for the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}
.

Lemma 5.1

Let
\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}
be the operator defined by (
5.4
), with
\Phi
satisfying (
1.33
). There exists
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{i}
satisfying (
1.8
) and (
1.9
), such that for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|(\chi_{i}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}H)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}((\chi_{i}H)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})\|_{\dot{C}^{l+\kappa}}\lesssim\|H\|_{C^{l+\kappa+1-\zeta_{0}}},

(5.7)

for some
\zeta_{0}\in(0,1)
.

Proof.
We divide the proof into two steps. In the first step, we reformulate
((\chi_{i}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}H)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})
and express it as the sum of the principal part
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}((\chi_{i}H)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})
and several error terms, where the principal operator satisfies (
1.8
) and (
1.9
). In the second step, we show that the error terms satisfy (
5.7
).

Step 1: Reformulation.

Recall that the initial data
X_{0}
satisfies

\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}:=\sup_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}|}{|X_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-X_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|}<\infty.

(5.8)

Since we choose
\|X_{0}-\Phi\|_{C^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
by (
1.33
), by (
5.8
) we obtain

\inf_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{|\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|}{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}|}\geq\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}^{-1}-2C\|X_{0}-\Phi\|_{C^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\geq\frac{4\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}^{-1}}{5},

(5.9)

provided
\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{1}{100(C+10)\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}}
.
Recall that we can split the integration region of (
5.4
) into the union of finite balls
\cup_{i=1}^{n}B(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},a_{1})
. Consider a small neighborhood of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}
, and define

\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}H=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\cap B(\hat{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},a_{2})}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\cdot(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}H)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}).

Denote
H_{i}=H\chi_{i}
,
h_{i}=H_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}
,
\phi_{i}=(\tilde{\chi}_{i}\Phi)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}
. Now we approximate
\chi_{i}(\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}H)
by
\tilde{\chi}_{i}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}H_{i}
, and denote the error term as

N_{4}=\chi_{i}(\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}H)-\tilde{\chi}_{i}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}H_{i},\quad\tilde{N}_{4}=N_{4}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}.

(5.10)

We consider
\tilde{\chi}_{i}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}H_{i}
in the following. We consider
\left(\widetilde{\nabla}G\cdot(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}(H\chi_{i}))\right)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}
. By changing variable formula, generally for any
F:\mathbb{S}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and
f=F\circ\mathcal{X}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}F(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{2}{1+|\eta|^{2}}\right)^{2}f(\eta)d\eta.

(5.11)

And by classical calculus, for any
F,G:\mathbb{S}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle(\tilde{\nabla}F\cdot\tilde{\nabla}G)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1+|\theta|^{2}}{2}\right)^{4}\sum_{j,l,m}\frac{\partial(F\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})}{\partial\theta_{l}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}^{j}}{\partial\theta_{l}}\frac{\partial(G\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})}{\partial\theta_{m}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}^{j}}{\partial\theta_{m}}

(5.12)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1+|\theta|^{2}}{2}\right)^{2}\nabla(F\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})\cdot\nabla(G\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}).

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{T}}(\theta,\beta)=\mathbf{T}(\rho(\theta)\beta)=\frac{\mathcal{T}(\rho(\theta)\beta)}{\rho(\theta)\beta},\ \ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\beta\in\mathbb{R}.

for some smooth
\rho
bounded from below and above, such that
\rho(\theta)=\frac{1+|\theta|^{2}}{2}
for
|\theta|\leq 4R_{0}
with
R_{0}
in (
5.1
). Moreover, corresponding to the tensor
J
in (
5.5
), we define the
3\times 3\times 2\times 2
tensor
\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})
as

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\right)_{kn}^{jl}=\sum_{\sigma=1,2}\left(\tilde{J}_{\sigma}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i}(\eta))\right)_{kn}^{jl},\quad l,n=1,2,\quad j,k=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{J}_{1}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i}(\eta))\right)_{kn}^{jl}=\tilde{\mathbf{T}}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i}(\eta))\delta_{ln}\delta_{jk},

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{J}_{2}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i}(\eta))\right)_{kn}^{jl}=(\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{T}}(\eta,\beta))\Big|_{\beta=|\nabla\phi_{i}(\eta)|}\frac{(\nabla\phi_{i}(\eta))_{jl}(\nabla\phi_{i}(\eta))_{kn}}{|\nabla\phi_{i}(\eta)|}.

By (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) we can write

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}G(\Phi_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\cdot(J((\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi))\widetilde{\nabla}H_{i})(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})\right)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G(\phi_{i}(\theta)-\phi_{i}(\eta))\nabla\cdot\left(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i}(t,\eta)\right)d\eta.

We approximate
G(\phi_{i}(\theta)-\phi_{i}(\eta))
by
G(\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))
, so we have

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\chi}_{i}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}H_{i}\right)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}(t,\theta)=\left(\tilde{\chi}_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}\right)(\theta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G(\phi_{i}(\theta)-\phi_{i}(\eta))\nabla\cdot\left(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i}(t,\eta)\right)d\eta

(5.13)

\displaystyle=\left(\tilde{\chi}_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}\right)(\theta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G(\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))\nabla\cdot\left(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i}(t,\eta)\right)d\eta+\tilde{N}_{5}(t,\theta),

with error term
\tilde{N}_{5}
defined as

\tilde{N}_{5}(t,\theta)=(\tilde{\chi}_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})(\theta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(G(\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))-G(\phi_{i}(\theta)-\phi_{i}(\eta))\right)\nabla\cdot(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i})(t,\eta)d\eta.

We proceed by analyzing the first term on the right-hand side of equation (
5.13
) using the methodology established in
[
61
]
. For
G(\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))
, denote

\displaystyle A(\theta)=\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)\in\mathbb{M}_{3\times 2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\quad B(\theta)=\sqrt{A^{\top}(\theta)A(\theta)}\in\mathbb{M}_{2\times 2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\quad Q(\theta)=A(\theta)B^{-1}(\theta)\in\mathbb{M}_{3\times 2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

where
\mathbb{M}_{m\times n}
denotes the set of
m\times n
matrices. By fundamental calculus, we know that

\displaystyle\left|A\eta\right|^{2}=(A\eta)^{\top}A\eta=\eta^{\top}(A^{\top}A)\eta=\left|B\eta\right|^{2},

\displaystyle(A\eta)\otimes(A\eta)=(A\eta)(A\eta)^{\top}=A\eta\eta^{\top}A^{\top}=QB\eta\eta^{\top}B^{\top}Q^{\top}=Q(B\eta)\otimes(B\eta)Q^{\top},

and

\mathcal{F}\left(\frac{1}{|\theta|}\right)(\xi)=\frac{2\pi}{|\xi|},\quad\mathcal{F}\left(\frac{\theta_{i}\theta_{j}}{|\theta|^{3}}\right)(\xi)=2\pi\left(\frac{\delta_{ij}}{|\xi|}-\frac{\xi_{i}\xi_{j}}{|\xi|^{3}}\right).

By changing variable, for any fixed
\theta
, we write
\mathcal{F}(G(\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)\cdot))(\xi)
as

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi\mathrm{Id}_{3}}{|\det B(\theta)||B^{-1}\xi|}+\frac{2\pi}{|\det B(\theta)|}Q\left(\frac{\mathrm{Id}_{2}}{|B^{-1}\xi|}-\frac{(B^{-1}\xi)\otimes(B^{-1}\xi)}{|B^{-1}\xi|^{3}}\right)Q^{\top}=2\pi\frac{\mathrm{Id}_{3}+v(\theta,\xi)\otimes v(\theta,\xi)}{|\det B(\theta)||B^{-1}\xi|}

(5.14)

with
v(\theta,\xi)=Q\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\frac{B^{-1}\xi}{|B^{-1}\xi|}
. By (
5.9
),
A
is non-degenerate and
B
is positive definite, with

0<(1+{\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}})C^{-1}\leq|\det B|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\|\Phi\|_{W^{1,\infty}},

where
C
is a universal constant. Consequently, the matrix defined in (
5.14
) is positive definite. More precisely, denote
C_{B,\xi}=\frac{1}{\det(B)|B^{-1}\xi|}
, then
C_{B,\xi}|\xi|
is uniformly bounded from above and below, and the eigenvalues of the matrix in (
5.14
) are positive.

We define
\tilde{\phi}_{i}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{3\times 2})
as an extension of
\nabla\phi_{i}
bounded up and below. Precisely, it satisfies

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{i}(\theta)\equiv\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta),\quad\text{for}\ |\theta|\leq 3R_{0},

\displaystyle 0<C_{1}<|\tilde{\phi}_{i}|<C_{2}<\infty,

\displaystyle\|{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}\|_{C^{m+2}}\leq C_{3}\|\Phi\|_{C^{m+2}}<\infty.

Based on (
5.14
) and following the calculations in Section 4 of
[
61
]
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{i}f(t,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G(\tilde{\phi}_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))\nabla\cdot\left(\tilde{J}(\theta,\tilde{\phi}_{i})\nabla f(t,\eta)\right)d\eta

(5.15)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{(\mathrm{Id}_{3}+\tilde{v}(\theta,\xi)\otimes\tilde{v}(\theta,\xi))\big(\mathcal{J}(\tilde{\phi}_{i})(\theta,\xi)\big)}{|\det B(\theta)||B^{-1}\xi|}\hat{f}(t,\xi)e^{i\theta\cdot\xi}d\xi,

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(\tilde{\phi}_{i})(\theta,\xi)=\frac{\mathcal{T}(\rho(\theta)|\tilde{A}|)}{\rho(\theta)|\tilde{A}|}\left(|\xi|^{2}\mathrm{Id}_{3}-\frac{(\tilde{A}\cdot\xi)\otimes(\tilde{A}\cdot\xi)}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\right)+\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(\rho(\theta)|\tilde{A}|)\frac{(\tilde{A}\cdot\xi)\otimes(\tilde{A}\cdot\xi)}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}},

(5.16)

\displaystyle\tilde{A}=\tilde{A}(\theta)=\tilde{\phi}_{i}(\theta),\quad\tilde{B}(\theta)=\sqrt{\tilde{A}^{\top}(\theta)\tilde{A}(\theta)},\quad\tilde{Q}(\theta)=\tilde{A}(\theta)\tilde{B}^{-1}(\theta),\quad\tilde{v}(\theta,\xi)=\tilde{Q}(\theta)\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\frac{\tilde{B}^{-1}\xi}{|\tilde{B}^{-1}\xi|}.

By invoking the result from
[
61
]
and using the fact that
\tilde{A}
has full rank while
\tilde{B}
is positive definite, along with condition (
1.27
) and positivity of (
5.14
), we deduce that the operator in (
5.15
) satisfies the lower bound
C\mathfrak{c}(R)|\xi|
where
C>0
,
R=2\|X_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}
and
\mathfrak{c}_{R}
is defined in (
1.27
). Furthermore, by the smoothness of
\tilde{\phi}_{i}
, an upper bound for the derivatives is easily verified, revealing (
1.8
) holds. We refer to Section 4,
[
61
]
for the proof of coercive condition (
1.9
) of the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{i}
. So we have

\left(\tilde{\chi}_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}\right)(\theta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G(\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))\nabla\cdot\left(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i}(t,\eta)\right)d\eta=\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{i}f(t,\theta)+\tilde{N}_{6}(t,\theta),

(5.17)

with the dominate operator
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{i}
satisfying (
1.8
) and (
1.9
), and the error term
\tilde{N}_{6}

\tilde{N}_{6}(t,\theta)=(\tilde{\chi}_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})(\theta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G(\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))\nabla\cdot(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i})(t,\eta)d\eta-\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{i}h_{i}(t,\theta).

Combining (
5.17
) with (
5.13
) and (
5.10
), we obtain

\displaystyle(\chi_{i}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}H)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}((\chi_{i}H)\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})=\tilde{N}_{4}+\tilde{N}_{5}+\tilde{N}_{6}.

Step 2: Error estimates.

To prove (
5.7
), it suffices to prove

\sum_{j=4,5,6}\|\tilde{N}_{j}(t)\|_{C^{l+\kappa}}\lesssim\|H(t)\|_{C^{l+\kappa+1-\zeta_{0}}},

(5.18)

for some small
\zeta_{0}\in(0,1)
and any
l\in\mathbb{N}
. In the following of the proof, we will shortly denote

M_{\Phi}:=(1+\mathbf{\Theta}_{0})\|\Phi\|_{C^{10m}},\quad m_{\Phi}:=\|\Phi\|_{C^{1}}.

(5.19)

By Lemma
6.5
, we only need to estimate
\|\tilde{N}_{j}\|_{C^{k}}
for any
k\leq m+1
.

For
\tilde{N}_{4}
, by definition of Hölder norm on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
and interpolation, we can write

\displaystyle N_{4}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})

\displaystyle=\tilde{\chi}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\chi_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\chi_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}(G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})))\cdot(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}H)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})

\displaystyle-\tilde{\chi}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\cdot(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)(\widetilde{\nabla}\chi_{i}\otimes H))(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})

\displaystyle:=N_{41}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})+N_{42}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}).

By (
5.9
), if we define
r=|\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}|
and the kernel
\mathbf{G}_{\Phi}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})=G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))
, then

r|(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})\mathbf{G}_{\Phi}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|+r^{2}|\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}\mathbf{G}_{\Phi}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|+r^{3}|\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}\mathbf{G}_{\Phi}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|\lesssim M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{2}.

Integrating by parts, we obtain that

\|N_{4}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})\|H(t)\|_{C^{1}}.

To estimate the Hölder norms, we employ the binomial identity

a^{k}=(a+b-b)^{k}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}C(k,l)(a+b)^{l}b^{k-l},

where
C(k,l)=(-1)^{k-l}\frac{k!}{l!(k-l)!}
is the refined binomial coefficient. This identity allows us to systematically transform derivatives from
\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}
-coordinates to the
\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}
-coordinates. Specifically, we apply this decomposition on both
\mathbb{S}^{2}
and
\mathbb{R}^{2}
to facilitate the norm estimates.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}^{k}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}C(k,l)(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{l}\tilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{k-l},

(5.20)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}^{k}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}C(k,l)(\nabla_{\theta}+\nabla_{\eta})^{l}\nabla_{\eta}^{k-l}.

Then by using (
5.9
), one can see that for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
r=|\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}|
,

\displaystyle r|(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{l}\mathbf{G}_{\Phi}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|

\displaystyle+r^{2}|\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{l}\mathbf{G}_{\Phi}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|

\displaystyle+r^{3}|\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{l}\mathbf{G}_{\Phi}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|\lesssim M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{l+2}.

Denote
\tilde{G}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})=\left(\chi_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\chi_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})\right)\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))
. For any
l\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle r|(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{l}\tilde{G}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|+r^{2}|\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{l}\tilde{G}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|\lesssim M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{l+2},

which together with (
5.20
) implies

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\nabla}^{k}N_{41}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m_{1}\leq k}\Big\|\widetilde{\nabla}^{m_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}^{k-m_{1}}(\tilde{G}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\cdot(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}H)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m_{1}\leq k}\Big\|\widetilde{\nabla}^{m_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{k-m_{1}}(\tilde{G}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\cdot(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}H)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{m_{1}\leq k-1}\Big\|\widetilde{\nabla}^{m_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})\sum_{1\leq m_{2}\leq k-m_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{k-m_{1}-m_{2}}(\tilde{G}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))

\displaystyle\times\left(\widetilde{\nabla}^{m_{2}-1}(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}H)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})-\widetilde{\nabla}^{m_{2}-1}(J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)\widetilde{\nabla}H)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})\right)d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k}}(1+\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k}})M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{k}\|H(t)\|_{C^{k+\varepsilon}},

for some
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Note that the critical norm here should be
\|H(t)\|_{C^{k+1}}
, which implies that
N_{41}
is a lower order term. For
\widetilde{\nabla}^{k}N_{42}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\nabla}^{k}N_{42}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\leq k\\
m_{2}\leq k-m_{1}\end{subarray}}\left\|\widetilde{\nabla}^{m_{1}}\tilde{\chi}_{i}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}})^{k-m_{1}-m_{2}}\widetilde{\nabla}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}}G(\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}))\right.

\displaystyle\times\left(\widetilde{\nabla}^{m_{2}}((J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)(\widetilde{\nabla}\chi_{i}\otimes H)))(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})-\widetilde{\nabla}^{m_{2}}((J(\widetilde{\nabla}\Phi)(\widetilde{\nabla}\chi_{i}\otimes H)))(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})\right)d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}}(1+\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}})M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{k+1}\|H(t)\|_{C^{k+\varepsilon}}.

From the above estimates, for any
0\leq k\leq m+1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|N_{4}(t)\|_{C^{k}}\lesssim M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{k+1}\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}}(1+\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}})\|H(t)\|_{C^{k+\varepsilon}}.

(5.21)

Now we estimate
\tilde{N}_{5}
. For
L^{\infty}
norm, it is straightforward to check that

\|\tilde{N}_{5}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim M_{\Phi}\|\nabla h_{i}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}.

For Hölder norms
\dot{C}^{k+1}
, we shortly denote
\chi_{i}^{R}(\theta)=(\chi_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})(\theta)
,
\tilde{\chi}_{i}^{R}(\theta)=(\tilde{\chi}_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})(\theta)
and
\tilde{G}(\theta,\eta)=G(\phi_{i}(\theta)-\phi_{i}(\eta))-G(\nabla\phi_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))
. By (
5.9
), we can see that

\displaystyle|\tilde{\chi}_{i}^{R}(\theta)\tilde{\chi}_{i}^{R}(\eta)\nabla_{\theta}\nabla_{\eta}\tilde{G}(\theta,\eta)|\lesssim\frac{M_{\Phi}}{|\theta-\eta|^{2}}.

Furthermore, we notice that for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle|\tilde{\chi}_{i}^{R}(\theta)\tilde{\chi}_{i}^{R}(\eta)\nabla_{\theta}\nabla_{\eta}(\nabla_{\theta}+\nabla_{\eta})^{l}\tilde{G}(\theta,\eta)|\lesssim\frac{M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{l+1}}{|\theta-\eta|^{2}}.

Applying (
5.20
) and integration by parts, similar to (
5.21
), we deduce

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}\tilde{N}_{5}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}}(1+\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}})^{k+1}M_{\Phi}\|h_{i}(t)\|_{C^{1}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq l\leq k\\
m\leq l-1\end{subarray}}\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k-l}}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{\theta}\nabla_{\eta}(\nabla_{\theta}+\nabla_{\eta})^{l-m-1}\tilde{G}(\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle\times\left(\nabla^{m}(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i})(t,\theta)-\nabla^{m}(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i})(t,\eta)\right)d\eta\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle+\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{\theta}\nabla_{\eta}\tilde{G}(\theta,\eta)\cdot\left(\nabla^{k-1}(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i})(t,\theta)-\nabla^{k-1}(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i})(t,\eta)\right)\tilde{\chi}_{i}(\eta)d\eta\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle+\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{G}(\theta,\eta)\cdot\nabla_{\eta}\tilde{\chi}_{i}(\eta)\nabla^{k-1}(\tilde{J}(\eta,\nabla\phi_{i})\nabla h_{i})(t,\theta)d\eta\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}}(1+\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}})^{k+1}M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{k+2}\|h_{i}(t)\|_{C^{k+\varepsilon}}.

The estimate above gives for any
0\leq k\leq m+1
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{N}_{5}(t)\|_{C^{k}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}}M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{k+1}\|h_{i}(t)\|_{C^{k+\varepsilon}}.

(5.22)

We write
\tilde{N}_{6}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{N}_{6}(t,\theta)=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(G(\tilde{\phi}_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta)))\nabla\cdot\left(\big(\tilde{J}(\eta,\tilde{\phi}_{i})-\tilde{J}(\theta,\tilde{\phi}_{i})\big)\nabla h_{i}(t,\eta)\right)d\eta

\displaystyle-(1-\tilde{\chi}_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})(\theta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G(\tilde{\phi}_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))\nabla\cdot\left(\tilde{J}(\eta,\tilde{\phi}_{i})\nabla h_{i}(t,\eta)\right)d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{N}_{61}(t,\theta)+\tilde{N}_{62}(t,\theta).

For
\tilde{N}_{61}
, we use similar methods as
\tilde{N}_{4}
and
\tilde{N}_{5}

to get

\displaystyle\|\tilde{N}_{61}(t)\|_{C^{k}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}}(1+\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}})^{k+1}M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{k+2}\|h_{i}(t)\|_{C^{k+\varepsilon}}.

For
\tilde{N}_{62}
, we notice that
\text{Supp}(1-\tilde{\chi}_{i}\circ\mathcal{R}_{i}\circ\mathcal{X})\subset\{|\theta|\geq 3R_{0}\}
, and
\text{Supp}(\nabla h_{i})\subset\{|\eta|\leq 2R_{0}\}
, so
\tilde{N}_{62}
has no singularity. Precisely, we have

\left|\nabla_{\theta}^{l}\left(G(\tilde{\phi}_{i}(\theta)(\theta-\eta))\right)\right|\lesssim(1+M_{\Phi})^{l+2}R_{0}^{-l},\quad\forall|\theta|\geq 3R_{0},\ |\eta|\leq 2R_{0}.

Then it follows

\|\tilde{N}_{62}(t)\|_{C^{k}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}}(1+\|\tilde{\chi}_{i}\|_{C^{k+1}})^{k+1}M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{k+2}R_{0}^{-l}\|h_{i}(t)\|_{C^{1}}.

We conclude that for any
0\leq k\leq m+1
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{N}_{6}(t)\|_{C^{k}}\lesssim M_{\Phi}(1+M_{\Phi})^{k+2}\|h_{i}(t)\|_{C^{k+\varepsilon}}.

(5.23)

Combining (
5.21
), (
5.22
) and (
5.23
), we obtain (
5.18
). This completes the proof of the lemma.

5.2
Proof of Theorem
1.10

In this section, we apply Theorem
2.12
(Schauder-type estimates on manifolds) to the reformulated system (
5.3
), along with the nonlinear estimates (Lemmas
6.20
–
6.22
) and the error estimate (Lemma
5.1
) to establish well-posedness results.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N},\kappa\in(0,1)
and
1-\kappa\ll 1
. Define the following norm and set,

\displaystyle\|X\|_{Z_{T}}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|X(t)\|_{C^{1}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|\tilde{\nabla}X(t)\|_{C^{m+\kappa}}\right),

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}=\left\{X:\mathbf{\Theta}_{X}(T)<2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0},\ \|X-\Phi\|_{Z_{T}}\leq\sigma\right\}.

Similarly to (
4.18
), we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}_{T}(X)=\left(C(1+\mathbf{\Theta}_{0})^{m+1}(\mathfrak{c}(\|X\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}W^{1,\infty}})^{-1}+\mathfrak{C}(\|X_{0}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}W^{1,\infty}}))\right)^{m+2},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{1}=\left(C(1+\mathbf{\Theta}_{0})^{m+1}(\mathfrak{c}(4\|X_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})^{-1}+\mathfrak{C}(4\|X_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}))\right)^{m+2},

where
C
is a universal constant and
\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{C}
defined in (
1.27
). Similar to (
4.19
), we obtain

\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}_{T}(X)\leq\mathfrak{M}_{1},\quad\forall X\in\mathcal{Z}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma},

provided
\sigma,\varepsilon_{0}<\min\{\frac{1}{100\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}},\frac{1}{2}\|X_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\}
.
Define the map
\mathcal{S}(Q)=F
to be the solution of

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(F-\Phi)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})+\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}(F-\Phi)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})=N(Q,\Phi)(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}),

(5.26)

\displaystyle F(0,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})=F_{0}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}),

with
C
a universal constant and
F_{0}=X_{0}
. We prove
\mathcal{S}
is a contraction map on
\mathcal{Z}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}
for some small
\sigma
and
T
.
For brevity, denote
H=F-\Phi
and
H_{0}=F_{0}-\Phi
.
With Lemma
5.1
, we are able to apply (
2.59
) in Theorem
2.12
to (
5.26
) with
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{M}}^{s}=\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{s,j}
as (
5.15
),
G=N(Q,\Phi)
,
n=1
. There exists
T>0
such that

\|H\|_{Z_{T}}\leq C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{1}\left(\|H_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}+\mathsf{Data}^{1,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathbb{S}^{2}}(N(Q,\Phi))\right),

where
\mathsf{Data}^{1,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathbb{S}^{2}}(N(Q,\Phi))
is defined in (
2.59
). Applying Lemma
6.20
, Lemma
6.21
and Lemma
6.22
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}^{1,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathbb{S}^{2}}(N(Q,\Phi))\leq C_{1}\mathfrak{M}_{1}(\|Q-\Phi\|_{Z_{T}}+T^{\kappa}M_{\Phi})^{2}(1+\|Q-\Phi\|_{Z_{T}}+TM_{\Phi})^{m+1}(1+\|Q-\Phi\|_{Z_{T}}+m_{\Phi})^{3},

where
M_{\Phi},m_{\Phi}
are defined in (
5.19
).
Furthermore, by (
5.9
) one has

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}t<T\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{|F(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-F(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|}{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}|}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{|\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-\Phi(\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|}{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}|}-2\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\nabla_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(F-\Phi)(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4}{3\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}}-2C_{2}\sigma.

Hence, for any
Q\in\mathcal{Z}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}
, we can take
\varepsilon_{0}<2^{-m-5}\mathfrak{M}_{1}^{-2}(1+C_{0}C_{1}+C_{2}\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}+m_{\Phi})^{-3}
,
\sigma=2C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{1}\varepsilon_{0}
and
T<{2^{-m-5}(C_{1}+M_{\Phi})^{-m-1}}\sigma
small enough such that

\displaystyle\|H\|_{Z_{T}}\leq C_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{1}\varepsilon_{0}+\frac{\sigma}{4}\leq\sigma,

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t<T\\
\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{|\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}}|}{|F(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})-F(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{y}})|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4}{3\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}}-2C_{2}\sigma\right)^{-1}\leq 2\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}.

So we have proved that
\mathcal{S}:\mathcal{Z}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}
. Now we only need to prove that
\mathcal{S}
has the contraction property. For
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}
, denote
H_{i}=\mathcal{S}Q_{i}
, and
\mathbf{H}=H_{1}-H_{2}
,
\mathbf{Q}=Q_{1}-Q_{2}
,
\mathbf{N}=N(Q_{1},\Phi)-N(Q_{2},\Phi)
and
\vec{Q}=(Q_{1},Q_{2})
. We write the equation of
\mathbf{H}
as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{H}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})+\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{N}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}(0,\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}})=0.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\|\mathbf{H}(t)\|_{C^{1}}+t^{m+\kappa}\|\mathbf{H}(t)\|_{C^{m+1+\kappa}})\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{1}\mathsf{Data}^{1,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mathbf{N}).

By Lemma
6.20
and Lemma
6.21
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Data}^{1,m,\kappa}_{T,\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mathbf{N})

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\mathfrak{M}_{1}\|\mathbf{Q}\|_{Z_{T}}(\|\vec{Q}-\Phi\|_{Z_{T}}+T^{\kappa}M_{\Phi})(1+\|\vec{Q}-\Phi\|_{Z_{T}}+T^{\kappa}M_{\Phi})^{m}(1+\|\vec{Q}-\Phi\|_{Z_{T}}+m_{\Phi})^{3}.

Taking
\varepsilon_{0}<2^{-10m}\mathfrak{M}_{1}^{-2}(1+C_{2}\mathbf{\Theta}_{0}+C_{3}+m_{\Phi})^{-3}
and
T<\frac{1}{100}(1+M_{\Phi})^{-10m}\sigma
be small enough, we obtain

\|\mathcal{S}(Q_{1})-\mathcal{S}(Q_{2})\|_{Z_{T}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{Q}\|_{Z_{T}}.

By contraction mapping theorem, there exists a unique
X\in\mathcal{Z}_{T,\Phi}^{\sigma}
such that
\mathcal{S}X=X
, which is a solution to (
1.29
). This completes the proof of Theorem
1.10
.

6
Appendix

6.1
Besov space and some interpolation inequalities

We first recall the definition of homogeneous Besov spaces.

Definition 6.1

Let
\varkappa
be a real number. The homogeneous Besov space
\dot{B}^{\varkappa}_{\infty,\infty}
consists of those distributions
u
such that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\dot{B}^{\varkappa}_{\infty,\infty}}:=\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{j\varkappa}\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}u\|_{L^{\infty}}<\infty.

Here
\{\dot{\Delta}_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
are the standard Littlewood-Paley decomposition blocks:
\dot{\Delta}_{j}f=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\phi_{j}(\xi)\hat{f}(\xi))
, where

\displaystyle\phi_{j}(\xi)=\phi(2^{-j}\xi),\ \ \operatorname{supp}(\phi)\subset\{\xi:\frac{3}{4}\leq|\xi|\leq\frac{8}{3}\},\ \ \sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\phi(2^{-j}\xi)\equiv 1.

Remark 6.2

•

For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have the equivalence:
\|\nabla u\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}}\sim\|u\|_{\dot{B}^{s+1}_{\infty,\infty}}
.

•

For
s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\backslash\mathbb{N}
, the Besov space
\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}
coincides with the homogeneous Hölder space
\dot{C}^{s}
. This equivalence fails when
s\in\mathbb{N}
.

•

The
\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}
norm admits the following equivalent characterization (see
[
100
]
):

\|u\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\sim\sup_{x,y}\frac{|2u(x)-u(x+y)-u(x-y)|}{|y|}.

Remark 6.3

[
100
]

Let
s,\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
. Then
(-\Delta)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}:\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}\to\dot{B}^{s-\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}
is an
isomorphism.

In the following proposition, we present a characterization of Besov space
\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,\infty}
that relies on the the fundamental solution defined by (
2.4
).

Proposition 6.4

For any
a>0
,
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, denote
\mathcal{K}(f)(t,x)=\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,0)\ast f
, where
\mathsf{K}_{x_{0}}(t,0,x)
is defined by (
2.4
).
Then there holds

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}t^{\frac{a}{s}}\|\mathcal{K}(f)(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,\infty}}.

The proof of Proposition
6.4
follows from Littlewood-Paley theory, see
[
12
]
for detail.

We list some elementary inequalities that used frequently throughout the paper.

Lemma 6.5

1. For any
0<\gamma_{1}<\gamma<\gamma_{2}
, there holds

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma_{1}}}^{\frac{\gamma_{2}-\gamma}{\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{1}}}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma_{2}}}^{\frac{\gamma-\gamma_{1}}{\gamma_{2}-\gamma_{1}}}.

2. For any
j,j_{1},j_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with
0\leq j_{1}\leq j\leq j_{2}
,
p\in[1,\infty]
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{j}f\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{j_{1}}f\|_{L^{p}}^{\frac{j_{2}-j}{j_{2}-j_{1}}}\|\nabla^{j_{2}}f\|_{L^{p}}^{\frac{j-j_{1}}{j_{2}-j_{1}}}.

3. Let
a\in(0,1)
, for any
f,g\in C^{a}
, there holds

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}+\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{a}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}.

4. (
[
25
, Lemma 2.2]
) Let
\theta_{1}\in(0,1)
. For any function
f
and any
0<\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{1}{2}\min\{\theta_{1},1-\theta_{1}\}
, there hold

\|\Lambda^{\theta_{1}}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\theta_{1}-\varepsilon_{0}}}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\theta_{1}+\varepsilon_{0}}})^{\frac{1}{2}},

\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\theta_{1}}}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{\theta_{1}}f\|_{L^{\infty}}.

We have the following interpolation lemma, which is classical in singular integral theory.

Lemma 6.6

i) Let
m(\xi)
be a Fourier multiplier of order
\sigma
with
\sigma>-d
, and

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\xi}^{k}m(\xi)|\leq|\xi|^{\sigma-k}.

(6.1)

Then for
\mathcal{T}f=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(m\hat{f})
, it holds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\|\Lambda^{\sigma+\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Lambda^{\sigma-\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(6.2)

for any
\varepsilon
small enough.

ii) Let
m(x,\xi)
be a Fourier multiplier of order
\sigma
with
\sigma>-d
, with the corresponding operator
\mathcal{T}
defined by

\mathcal{T}f=(2\pi)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\hat{f}(\xi)m(x,\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}d\xi,

and satisfies (
6.1
) for any
x
, then (
6.2
) also holds.

Proof.
i) Denote
\chi_{\ell}
as a dilation of a non-negative smooth cut-off function
\chi_{\ell}(\xi)=\chi(\frac{\xi}{\ell})
,
\mathbf{1}_{|\xi|<1}\leq\chi\leq\mathbf{1}_{|\xi|>2}
. Then we have the decomposition

\mathcal{T}f=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\chi_{\ell}\frac{m(\cdot)}{|\cdot|^{\sigma-\varepsilon}}\widehat{\Lambda^{\sigma-\varepsilon}f}\right)+\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left((1-\chi_{\ell})\frac{m(\cdot)}{|\cdot|^{\sigma+\varepsilon}}\widehat{\Lambda^{\sigma+\varepsilon}f}\right).

By classical result in singular integral theory, one can see that

\left\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\chi_{\ell}\frac{m(\cdot)}{|\cdot|^{\sigma-\varepsilon}}\right)\right\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\ell^{\varepsilon},\quad\left\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\chi_{\ell}\frac{m(\cdot)}{|\cdot|^{\sigma+\varepsilon}}\right)\right\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\ell^{-\varepsilon}.

We can take
\ell^{\varepsilon}=\|\Lambda^{\sigma+\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\Lambda^{\sigma-\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-1}
to get the result.

ii) Define
\mathcal{T}_{y}f(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(m(y,\cdot)\hat{u})(x)
. Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, there holds

\left|\mathcal{T}f(x)\right|\lesssim\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\mathcal{T}_{x}f(y)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{\sigma+\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Lambda^{\sigma-\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we use (
6.2
) for the last inequality.

Lemma 6.7

Let
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in(0,1)
, for any function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
, there holds

\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}f\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa_{1}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa_{1}}},\quad\quad\quad\quad\sup_{\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\|\delta_{\alpha}\delta_{\beta}f\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa_{1}}|\beta|^{\kappa_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}}.

Denote
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g(x)=\frac{\delta_{\alpha}\delta_{-\alpha}h(x)}{|\alpha|}
. Then for any
\nu\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
a\in(0,1)
, there hold

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1+\nu}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1-\nu}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\delta_{\alpha}f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\delta_{\alpha}g\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{d\alpha}{|\alpha|^{d+1}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\nu}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\frac{1}{2}-\nu}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover, for
\mathsf{E}^{\alpha},\mathsf{E}_{\alpha}
defined in (
6.11
),
\tilde{\mathsf{E}}^{\alpha}
defined in (
4.5
), and
h:\mathbb{S}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
, there hold

\displaystyle\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\|\mathsf{E}^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathsf{E}_{\alpha}f\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa_{1}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{1+\kappa_{1}}},\quad\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\|\tilde{\mathsf{E}}^{\alpha}h\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|\alpha|^{\kappa_{1}}}\lesssim\|h\|_{\dot{C}^{1+\kappa_{1}}}.

Lemma 6.8

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Consider
g,g_{1},g_{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, and
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m+2}\|f^{(k)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1.

Then

\|\nabla^{m}(f\circ g)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m}+\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m}},

(6.3)

\|\nabla^{m}(f\circ g)\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{m+\alpha}{\alpha}}+\|g\|_{\dot{C}^{m+\alpha}},

(6.4)

\|\nabla^{m}(f\circ g_{1}-f\circ g_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{m}\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n}}(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m-n}+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}}),

(6.5)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{m}(f\circ g_{1}-f\circ g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{m}\left\{\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n+\alpha}}(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m-n}+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}})\right.

(6.6)

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad+\|g_{1}-g_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{n}}(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{m-n+\alpha}{\alpha}}+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n+\alpha}})\right\}.

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{m}\big((f\circ g_{1}-f\circ g_{2})-(f\circ g_{3}-f\circ g_{4})\big)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

(6.7)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=0}^{m}\|(g_{1}-g_{2})-(g_{3}-g_{4})\|_{\dot{C}^{n}}(\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{m-n}+\|(g_{1},g_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{m-n}})

\displaystyle+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}=n}\|g_{3}-g_{4}\|_{\dot{C}^{n_{1}}}\|(g_{1}-g_{3},g_{2}-g_{4})\|_{\dot{C}^{n_{2}}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{4}(\|g_{k}\|_{\dot{C}^{1}}^{n_{3}}+\|g_{k}\|_{\dot{C}^{n_{3}}})\right),

Proof.
The first two inequalities follow from Lemma
6.5
, and we omit the proof.
For (
6.5
) and (
6.6
), since

f\circ g_{1}-f\circ g_{2}=\int_{0}^{1}(g_{1}-g_{2})\cdot\nabla f(g_{1}-\lambda(g_{1}-g_{2}))d\lambda.

We can get the result by (
6.3
), (
6.4
) and Lemma
6.5
,.

For the last two inequalities, similarly,

\displaystyle(f\circ g_{1}-f\circ g_{2})-(f\circ g_{3}-f\circ g_{4})
